    - <a id="rule_look_at_controlled_facedown"></a>**a.** A player may look at facedown cards they control at any time.
- <a id="rule_reveal"></a>**1.21.3.** To **reveal** a card is to show its front face to all players, then return it to its previous state.
    - <a id="rule_reveal_not_turn_faceup"></a>**a.** Revealing a facedown card does not count as turning it faceup.
- <a id="rule_expose"></a>**1.21.4.** Some abilities instruct the Runner to **expose** one or more cards. To expose a card is to reveal it, except that only installed, unrezzed cards can be exposed.
- <a id="rule_look_reveal_expose_access_distinct"></a>**1.21.5.** Effects that instruct a player to look at, reveal, expose, or access a card are not the same, even if they would be performed similarly at times. See [section 7](#chpt_access_breach) for more information on accessing.
- <a id="rule_remain_visible"></a>**1.21.6.** If a resolving ability directs one or both players to look at or reveal a card or set of cards, each such card remains visible to the relevant player(s) until the entire ability is finished resolving or the card moves to a different location. See also [rule 7.3.1a](#rule_visibility_after_access).
- <a id="rule_ambush_text"></a>**1.21.7.** The ability "While the Runner is accessing this [type] in R&D, they must reveal it." is a static ability that applies for the entire duration of the access. This is also the case for cards with the older wording, "If [this card] is accessed from R&D, the Runner must reveal it."

# <a id="chpt_parts_of_a_card"></a>2. Parts of a Card

## <a id="sec_card_name"></a>2.1. Name

- <a id="rule_card_name_definition"></a>**2.1.1.** The identifier of a card is its **name**, alternatively referred to as its title. It is written along the top of the card for identities, agendas, ice, operations, events, and resources, or immediately below the art box in the case of assets, upgrades, hardware, and programs.
- <a id="rule_identity_subtitles"></a>**2.1.2.** Identities have a subtitle written immediately below the main title line. Their full name is the combination of their main title and subtitle.
    - <a id="rule_identity_pronouns"></a>**a.** Runner identities may have the pronouns of the character represented by that identity written next to the subtitle. Pronouns do not affect gameplay.
- <a id="rule_self_reference"></a>**2.1.3.** When a card refers to its own name, it is a self-reference. See [rule 10.1.5](#sec_old_self_reference_rules).
- <a id="rule_copies_of"></a>**2.1.4.** When a card refers to "copies of" cards with a particular name, it refers to any card with that name.
- <a id="rule_act_on_different_names"></a>**2.1.5.** If a player is directed to choose or search for cards "with different names", each card chosen or found by the search must have a different English name from every other card chosen or found. See also [section 8.7](#sec_searching_cards).

## <a id="sec_unique_symbol"></a>2.2. Unique Symbol

- <a id="rule_unique_symbol"></a>**2.2.1.** Some cards are **unique**, and have a unique symbol (◆) before their name to designate this. See [rule 10.1.1](#rule_uniqueness).

## <a id="sec_printed_cost"></a>2.3. Play Cost, Install Cost, or Rez Cost

- <a id="rule_printed_cost_definition"></a>**2.3.1.** The play, install, or rez cost of a card is a number that appears in the top-left corner of most card types. Play and install costs appear within a large circular icon, while rez costs appear within a small octagonal icon.
- <a id="rule_paying_printed_costs"></a>**2.3.2.** Play, install, and rez costs on cards are all paid in credits. [Section 8](#chpt_card_manipulation) details how cards are played, installed, and rezzed.
- <a id="rule_card_types_with_play_costs"></a>**2.3.3.** Events and operations have a play cost.
- <a id="rule_card_types_with_install_costs"></a>**2.3.4.** Hardware, programs, and resources have an install cost.
    - <a id="rule_ice_have_install_costs_too"></a>**a.** Ice also has an install cost, but it does not appear on the card. The install cost of ice is determined by the configuration of other installed ice rather than being intrinsic to the card. See [section 1.16.6](#rule_install_cost).
- <a id="rule_card_types_with_rez_costs"></a>**2.3.5.** Assets, ice, and upgrades have a rez cost.

## <a id="sec_adv_req"></a>2.4. Advancement Requirement

- <a id="rule_adv_requirement_location"></a>**2.4.1.** The advancement requirement appears only on agendas. It is a number in the top-right corner of the card, within a small circle.
- <a id="rule_adv_requirement_citation"></a>**2.4.2.** [Section 1.17](#sec_score_scoring_steal) details how agendas are scored and the purpose of the advancement requirement.

## <a id="sec_agenda_points"></a>2.5. Agenda Points

- <a id="rule_agenda_points_location"></a>**2.5.1.** The agenda point value appears only on agendas. It is the number in the center-left of the card, on top of a graphic depicting three pillars.
- <a id="rule_agenda_points_citation"></a>**2.5.2.** The agenda points in a player's score area contribute toward that player winning the game. See [section 1.7](#sec_ending_the_game).

## <a id="sec_trash_cost"></a>2.6. Trash Cost

- <a id="rule_trash_cost_location"></a>**2.6.1.** Assets, upgrades, and some ice and operations have trash costs. A trash cost is a number appearing in the lower-right corner of the card within a [trash-cost] symbol.
- <a id="rule_trash_cost_citation"></a>**2.6.2.** [Section 7.1.5](#rule_basic_trash_ability) details how the Runner can trash a card they access by paying its trash cost.

## <a id="sec_strength"></a>2.7. Strength

- <a id="rule_strength_location"></a>**2.7.1.** Strength is a number appearing on ice and on *icebreaker* programs. It appears on the bottom-left corner of the card within a large semicircle.
- <a id="rule_strength_dash"></a>**2.7.2.** Non-*icebreaker* programs have a dash (‒) in the bottom-left corner instead of a strength value.
- <a id="rule_strength_citation"></a>**2.7.3.** [Section 3.9.5](#subsec_icebreakers) discusses the interaction between ice strength and *icebreaker* strength.

## <a id="sec_memory_cost"></a>2.8. Memory Cost

- <a id="rule_memory_cost_location"></a>**2.8.1.** Memory Cost is a number appearing only on programs. It appears to the right of the install cost, within an [mu] symbol.
- <a id="rule_memory_cost_citation"></a>**2.8.2.** [Section 1.20](#sec_memory) discusses the topic of memory, and [section 3.9.3](#rule_program_memory_cost) details how programs interact with the Runner's memory limit based on their memory cost.

## <a id="sec_base_link"></a>2.9. Base Link

- <a id="rule_base_link_location"></a>**2.9.1.** Base link is a number appearing near the top-left of Runner identity cards, next to the [link] symbol.
- <a id="rule_base_link_citation"></a>**2.9.2.** The Runner's base link contributes to their link value. [Section 10.7](#sec_link) defines how the Runner's link value is calculated, and [section 10.8](#sec_traces) explains how it contributes to contesting trace attempts.

## <a id="sec_memory_limit"></a>2.10. Starting Memory Limit

- <a id="rule_memory_limit_location"></a>**2.10.1.** Starting memory limit is a number appearing near the top-left of Runner identity cards, within the [mu] symbol.
- <a id="rule_memory_limit_citation"></a>**2.10.2.** The Runner's memory limit is checked against the total memory cost of programs they have installed. This value is calculated by applying any active modifiers to the Runner's starting memory limit. See [section 1.20](#sec_memory).

## <a id="sec_min_deck_size"></a>2.11. Minimum Deck Size

- <a id="rule_min_deck_size_location"></a>**2.11.1.** Minimum deck size is a number shown as the uppermost of two small boxes in the lower-left of Corp identities and the lower-right of Runner identities.
- <a id="rule_min_deck_size_citation"></a>**2.11.2.** [Section 1.4](#sec_deck_construction) details the rules for building a deck, including the requirement to adhere to the minimum deck size of the deck's associated identity.

## <a id="sec_inf_limit"></a>2.12. Influence Limit

- <a id="rule_inf_limit_location"></a>**2.12.1.** The influence limit is a number shown as the lowermost of two small boxes in the lower-left of Corp identities and the lower-right of Runner identities.
- <a id="rule_inf_limit_citation"></a>**2.12.2.** [Section 1.4](#sec_deck_construction) details the rules for building a deck, including the requirement that out-of-faction cards in the deck adhere to the influence limit of the associated identity.

## <a id="sec_faction"></a>2.13. Faction Affiliation

- <a id="rule_faction_list"></a>**2.13.1.** Cards are divided into ten factions: four Corp factions, three Runner factions, and three Runner mini-factions. Factions and influence restrict deckbuilding options, allowing each faction to have distinct play differences from one another.
    - <a id="rule_corp_faction_list"></a>**a.** The Corp factions are Haas-Bioroid, Jinteki, NBN, and the Weyland Consortium.
    - <a id="rule_runner_major_faction_list"></a>**b.** The major Runner factions are Anarch, Criminal, and Shaper.
    - <a id="rule_runner_minifaction_list"></a>**c.** The Runner mini-factions are Adam, Apex, and Sunny Lebeau.
- <a id="rule_faction_identification"></a>**2.13.2.** A card's faction can be identified by the color of its background, as well as a faction logo in one of its four corners. This logo also appears as a watermark behind the text box. If a card has a white background and no logo, it is neutral.
- <a id="rule_faction_citation"></a>**2.13.3.** Each identity is associated with one faction. [Section 1.4](#sec_deck_construction) details how factions affect deckbuilding.

## <a id="sec_inf_cost"></a>2.14. Influence Cost

- <a id="rule_inf_cost_location"></a>**2.14.1.** A card's influence cost, or influence value, is represented in a bar with 5 circular slots near the bottom of most cards. The influence cost is the number of slots that are filled with a blue circle, and can range from 0 to 5.
- <a id="rule_inf_cost_absent"></a>**2.14.2.** Some cards cannot be played out of faction, and therefore do not have an influence value, which is different from an influence cost of "0". These cards do not have the influence bar at all.
- <a id="rule_alliance"></a>**2.14.3.** Some Corp cards have the subtype *alliance* and text defining adjustments to their influence costs. The ability adjusting the influence cost of an *alliance* card applies only when determining the legality of an already constructed deck and does not have any effect during gameplay. The ability references other cards included in the deck to determine whether the card should apply its printed influence cost or 0 against the deck's influence limit.
- <a id="rule_inf_cost_citation"></a>**2.14.4.** [Section 1.4](#sec_deck_construction) details how a card's influence cost (or lack thereof) affects deckbuilding.

## <a id="sec_card_type"></a>2.15. Card Type

- <a id="rule_type_line_location"></a>**2.15.1.** A card's types appear as a line of text across the center of non-ice cards above the text box. For ice, they run along the left side of the text box, rotated 90° from the rest of the text.
- <a id="rule_card_type_list"></a>**2.15.2.** Each card has one card type, which can be identity, agenda, asset, ice, operation, upgrade, event, hardware, program, or resource. It is written in bold at the beginning of the type line.
- <a id="rule_card_type_while_inactive"></a>**2.15.3.** While inactive, a card is still considered a card of the type printed on it, with the exception of facedown installed Runner cards. See [section 8.1.4](#sec_facedown_runner_cards).
- <a id="rule_card_type_conversion_citation"></a>**2.15.4.** Abilities can change a card from its printed type to an agenda. See [rule 10.1.3](#rule_add_card_to_score_area).
- <a id="rule_card_type_counters"></a>**2.15.5.** Counters never have card types.

## <a id="sec_subtypes"></a>2.16. Subtypes

- <a id="rule_subtypes"></a>**2.16.1.** Cards may have one or more subtypes, which are written following the primary type. Subtypes are card descriptors that can be referenced by card abilities. Cards that share a subtype often share a distinct characteristic or ability.
    - <a id="rule_subtype_special_rules"></a>**a.** The subtypes *region*, *console*, and *icebreaker* have special rules, discussed in sections [3.6.5](#subsec_regions), [3.8.5](#subsec_consoles) and [3.9.5](#subsec_icebreakers) respectively. Other subtypes have no intrinsic rules meaning.
- <a id="rule_subtype_bold"></a>**2.16.2.** In card text, subtypes are always referenced in bold text. If a word or phrase that is also a subtype appears in a card's text but is not in bold text, that word or phrase does not reference the subtype.
- <a id="rule_lack_of_subtype"></a>**2.16.3.** Some abilities reference cards by a lack of subtype, such as "non-*virus*". These abilities can only affect cards that do not have the specified subtype, regardless of any other subtypes the cards do have.
- <a id="rule_subtypes_active"></a>**2.16.4.** A card has all of its subtypes at all times, even while inactive, with the exception of facedown installed Runner cards. See [section 8.1.4](#sec_facedown_runner_cards).
- <a id="rule_add_remove_subtypes"></a>**2.16.5.** Abilities can add or remove subtypes. The number of times a card has, gains, and loses each subtype is tracked as a running total, but the result is binary: if the total number of instances of the subtype exceeds the number of times it is removed, the card has that subtype. Otherwise, it does not.
    - > *Example:* The Runner plays Tinkering on a Lycan hosting 1 advancement counter. Lycan has two instances of *sentry*, one printed and one from Tinkering, and has lost one instance of *sentry* from its own ability. Thus it is still a *sentry*.
- <a id="rule_choose_valid_subtype"></a>**2.16.6.** If an effect requires a player to choose a subtype, that player must choose a single subtype that has been printed on at least one card. If a player is required to choose a subtype belonging to a specific card type, they must choose a subtype that has been printed on at least one card with that type.
- <a id="rule_subtypes_list"></a>**2.16.7.** Below is a list of all subtypes.
    - <a id="rule_identity_subtypes"></a>**a.** The identity subtypes are Bioroid, Clone, Corp, Cyborg, Department, Digital, Division, G-mod, Liability, Megacorp, Natural, Police Department, Stealth, Subsidiary, and Unsubstantiated.
    - <a id="rule_agenda_subtypes"></a>**b.** The agenda subtypes are Ambush, Assassination, Expansion, Expendable, Initiative, Liability, NEXT, Psi, Public, Research, Security, Sensie, and Source.
    - <a id="rule_asset_subtypes"></a>**c.** The asset subtypes are Academic, Advertisement, Alliance, Ambush, Beanstalk, Bioroid, Cast, Character, Clone, Corporation, Deep Net, Enforcer, Executive, Facility, Government, Hostile, Industrial, Liability, Political, Psi, Region, Research, Ritzy, Seedy, and Transaction.
    - <a id="rule_ice_subtypes"></a>**d.** The ice subtypes are AP, Advertisement, Ambush, Barrier, Bioroid, Code Gate, Deep Net, Deflector, Destroyer, Expendable, Grail, Harmonic, Liability, Morph, Mythic, NEXT, Observer, Psi, Sentry, Tracer, and Trap.
    - <a id="rule_operation_subtypes"></a>**e.** The operation subtypes are Alliance, Black Ops, Condition, Current, Double, Gray Ops, Liability, Lockdown, Mandate, Psi, Reprisal, Terminal, Transaction, and Triple.
    - <a id="rule_upgrade_subtypes"></a>**f.** The upgrade subtypes are Academic, Advertisement, Alliance, Ambush, Beanstalk, Bioroid, Character, Clone, Enforcer, Executive, Expendable, Facility, Hostile, Off-site, Orgcrime, Psi, Region, Ritzy, Seedy, Security Protocol, Sysop, and Unorthodox.
    - <a id="rule_event_subtypes"></a>**g.** The event subtypes are Condition, Current, Double, Job, Mod, Orgcrime, Priority, Run, Sabotage, Stealth, and Terminal.
    - <a id="rule_hardware_subtypes"></a>**h.** The hardware subtypes are Chip, Companion, Console, Consumer-grade, Cybernetic, Gear, Link, Mod, Stealth, Vehicle, and Weapon.
    - <a id="rule_program_subtypes"></a>**i.** The program subtypes are AI, Caïssa, Cloud, Daemon, Decoder, Deep Net, Deva, Fracter, Icebreaker, Killer, Stealth, Trojan, Virus, and Weapon.
    - <a id="rule_resource_subtypes"></a>**j.** The resource subtypes are Clan, Clone, Companion, Connection, Directive, Genetics, Government, Job, Link, Location, Remote, Ritzy, Sabotage, Seedy, Source, Stealth, and Virtual.

## <a id="sec_text_box"></a>2.17. Text Box

- <a id="rule_text_box_definition"></a>**2.17.1.** The **text box** is the central area on a card containing any abilities the card has. Abilities usually only apply when the card they appear on is active. [Section 9.1.8](#rule_ability_active_inactive_source_card) details exceptions.
- <a id="rule_flavor_text"></a>**2.17.2.** Many cards have italicised text written after their game text. This is **flavor text**, and has no effect on the game or the function of the card, nor can other abilities reference or interact with it.
- <a id="rule_reminder_text"></a>**2.17.3.** Some cards have italicised text in parentheses. This is **reminder text**, and has no effect on the game or the function of the card, nor can other abilities reference or interact with it directly. Reminder text simply summarizes a rule that may apply to that card text.

## <a id="sec_server_indicator"></a>2.18. Server Indicator

- <a id="rule_server_back"></a>**2.18.1.** One Corp identity, Méliès U, has several versions of its back face with different subtitles, and each of those versions features an additional icon next its the faction icon to aid in distinguishing the versions. The icons each represent a particular central server referred to in the trigger condition of the card's first ability.
- <a id="rule_server_icon"></a>**2.18.2.** The 3 server indicator icons are: an office building (representing HQ), a laboratory flask (representing R&D), and a computer server (representing Archives).
- <a id="sec_server_indicator_no_gameplay"></a>**2.18.3.** The server indicator is a visual aid only and has no direct gameplay function.

# <a id="chpt_card_types"></a>3. Card Types

## <a id="sec_identities"></a>3.1. Identities

- <a id="rule_identity_play_area"></a>**3.1.1.** Each player starts the game with a single identity active in the play area. See [section 1.6](#sec_starting_the_game).
    - <a id="rule_identity_double_sided"></a>**a.** Some identities are double-sided. Only the front side of a double-sided identity will have values for its minimum deck size and influence limit. Only the side of a double-sided identity that is currently faceup is active at any given time.
    - <a id="rule_identity_not_installed"></a>**b.** Identities are not installed.
- <a id="rule_identity_deck_size_influence"></a>**3.1.2.** Only identity cards have minimum deck sizes and influence limits. These values do not apply during the game; instead, they determine deckbuilding constraints for decks associated with that identity. See [section 1.4](#sec_deck_construction).
- <a id="rule_identity_base_link"></a>**3.1.3.** Only Runner identities have a base link. This value is used in calculating the Runner's link value. See [section 10.7](#sec_link).
- <a id="rule_identity_HQ"></a>**3.1.4.** The Corp's identity card is used to mark the location of HQ. See [section 4.3.8](#subsec_hq).

## <a id="sec_agendas"></a>3.2. Agendas

- <a id="rule_agendas_in_roots"></a>**3.2.1.** The Corp may install agendas only in the roots of remote servers. There can be only one agenda or one asset installed in the root of any given remote server at a time. See [section 4.6.8](#subsec_remote_servers).
- <a id="rule_agenda_advancement_requirement_agenda_points"></a>**3.2.2.** Only agendas have advancement requirements and agenda points. See [section 1.17](#sec_score_scoring_steal).
    - <a id="rule_agenda_advancement_requirement"></a>**a.** An agenda's advancement requirement is the minimum number of advancement counters that must be on that agenda for the Corp to score it.
    - <a id="rule_score_from_score_area"></a>**b.** The agenda points on an agenda contribute to a player's score while the agenda is in their score area.
- <a id="rule_agendas_not_rezzed"></a>**3.2.3.** Agendas cannot be rezzed and are inactive while installed. Agendas are active in the Corp's score area. Agendas are inactive in the Runner's score area unless the agenda's card text specifies otherwise. See [section 4.5](#sec_score_area).
    - <a id="rule_agendas_faceup"></a>**a.** Some agendas may be installed faceup or turned faceup through card abilities. Faceup agendas are neither rezzed nor unrezzed. If an agenda's printed text directs the Corp to install it faceup, that agenda's abilities are active while it is installed.
    - <a id="rule_advancemenet_requirements_modification"></a>**b.** Some agendas modify the requirements for stealing or scoring them. These abilities are always active, according to any restrictions or requirements specified. See [section 9.1.8](#rule_ability_active_inactive_source_card).
- <a id="rule_agendas_advanced"></a>**3.2.4.** Agendas can always be advanced. See [section 1.18](#sec_advancing_cards).

## <a id="sec_assets"></a>3.3. Assets

- <a id="rule_asset_root_remote"></a>**3.3.1.** The Corp may install assets only in the roots of remote servers. There can be only one agenda or one asset installed in the root of any given remote server at a time. See [section 4.6.8](#subsec_remote_servers).
- <a id="rule_asset_rez_cost"></a>**3.3.2.** Assets are among the card types that have rez costs. They are installed unrezzed and remain inactive until the Corp rezzes them by paying their rez costs during a paid ability window. See [section 8.1](#sec_faceup_facedown_status).
- <a id="rule_asset_trash_cost"></a>**3.3.3.** Assets are among the card types that have trash costs. If the Runner accesses an asset, they may pay the trash cost to trash that asset. See [section 7.1.5](#rule_basic_trash_ability).
- <a id="rule_asset_lady_liberty"></a>**3.3.4.** One asset, Lady Liberty, has the subtype *region* and the text "Limit 1 *region* per server." See [section 3.6.5](#subsec_regions) for rules governing *region* cards.

## <a id="sec_ice"></a>3.4. Ice

- <a id="rule_ice_piece_of_ice"></a>**3.4.1.** "Ice" and "piece of ice" are synonymous.
- <a id="rule_ice_install"></a>**3.4.2.** Ice are the only type of card installed protecting servers. Ice are not installed in the roots of servers. See [section 4.6.9](#subsec_protecting_a_server).
    - <a id="rule_ice_layout"></a>**a.** Ice protecting a server are laid out horizontally in front of that server, with the outermost piece of ice furthest from the server.
    - <a id="rule_ice_instal_outermost"></a>**b.** When the Corp installs a piece of ice, they must install it in the outermost position protecting a server. See [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
- <a id="rule_ice_rez_cost"></a>**3.4.3.** Ice are among the card types that have rez costs. They are installed unrezzed and remain inactive until the Corp rezzes them by paying their rez costs. See [section 8.1](#sec_faceup_facedown_status).
    - <a id="rule_ice_rez_window"></a>**a.** Unlike assets and upgrades, ice can normally only be rezzed while being approached during a run. See [section 6.4](#sec_approach_ice_phase).
- <a id="rule_ice_strength_value"></a>**3.4.4.** Ice are among the card types that have strength. An ice's strength determines which icebreakers are allowed to interface with it. See [section 3.9.5](#subsec_icebreakers).
    - <a id="rule_ice_strength_modification_duration"></a>**a.** If an ability modifies the strength of a piece of ice for the remainder of a run, and that ability resolves during an encounter outside of a run, the modification instead lasts for the remainder of that encounter.
        - > *Example:* The Runner accesses and encounters an Archangel in HQ with Gang Sign. The Runner uses Devil Charm to lower the strength of Archangel. That strength reduction would normally last for the remainder of the current run, but since no run is in progress, it instead lasts for the remainder of the encounter.
- <a id="rule_ice_trash_cost"></a>**3.4.5.** Some ice have trash costs. If the Runner accesses an ice with a trash cost, they may pay the trash cost to trash that ice. See [section 7.1.5](#rule_basic_trash_ability).
    - <a id="rule_ice_access"></a>**a.** Unlike assets, upgrades, and agendas, ice are not normally accessed while installed. Encountering a piece of ice does not entail accessing that ice and does not give the Runner an opportunity to pay its trash cost.
- <a id="rule_ice_install_cost"></a>**3.4.6.** Ice are the only Corp cards with install costs. The install cost of a piece of ice is not a printed value but instead a calculated value based on the number of other ice already protecting the relevant server. See [section 1.16.6](#rule_install_cost).
- <a id="rule_ice_subroutine"></a>**3.4.7.** Ice are the only cards with subroutines. See [section 9.8](#sec_subroutines).

## <a id="sec_operations"></a>3.5. Operations

- <a id="rule_operation_play"></a>**3.5.1.** Operations are the only Corp cards that are played. Operations are never installed. Playing an operation causes its abilities to become active, and once those abilities are fully resolved the operation is trashed. See [section 8.6](#sec_playing_events_and_operations).
    - <a id="rule_operation_condition"></a>**a.** Some operations have the subtype *condition*. As part of their abilities, *condition* operations convert themselves into *condition* counters and install themselves. A *condition* operation cannot be installed by an install effect; it can only be installed through its own conversion ability. See [rule 10.1.4](#rule_card_to_counter).
    - <a id="rule_operation_current"></a>**b.** Some operations have the subtype *current*. As part of their abilities, *current* operations prevent themselves from being trashed until another *current* operation or event is played or the Runner steals an agenda. When one of these occurs, the active *current* is trashed during the next checkpoint. See [rule 8.6.6c](#rule_play_not_trashed_until).
    - <a id="rule_operation_lockdown"></a>**c.** Some operations have the subtype *lockdown*. As part of their abilities, *lockdown* operations prevent themselves from being trashed until the start of the Corp's next turn after the operation is played. Once the Corp's turn begins, this effect expires and the operation is trashed during the next checkpoint, before any conditional abilities marked pending at that time can be triggered. See [rule 8.6.6c](#rule_play_not_trashed_until).
- <a id="rule_operation_play_cost"></a>**3.5.2.** Operations are the only Corp cards with play costs. An operation's play cost is the number of credits the Corp must spend to play the operation. See [section 8.6](#sec_playing_events_and_operations).
- <a id="rule_operation_trash_cost"></a>**3.5.3.** Some operations have trash costs. If the Runner accesses an operation with a trash cost, they may pay the trash cost to trash that operation. See [section 7.1.5](#rule_basic_trash_ability).

## <a id="sec_upgrades"></a>3.6. Upgrades

- <a id="rule_upgrade_install"></a>**3.6.1.** The Corp may install upgrades in the root of any server. Upgrades are the only card type that can be installed in the root of a central server.
- <a id="rule_upgrade_no_limit"></a>**3.6.2.** There is no limit to the number of upgrades that can be installed in the root of a single server.
- <a id="rule_upgrade_rez_cost"></a>**3.6.3.** Upgrades are among the card types that have rez costs. They are installed unrezzed and remain inactive until the Corp rezzes them by paying their rez costs during a paid ability window. See [section 8.1](#sec_faceup_facedown_status).
- <a id="rule_upgrade_trash_cost"></a>**3.6.4.** Upgrades are among the card types that have trash costs. If the Runner accesses an upgrade, they may pay the trash cost to trash that upgrade. See [section 7.1.5](#rule_basic_trash_ability).
- <a id="subsec_regions"></a>**3.6.5.** ***Regions***
    - <a id="rule_upgrade_region"></a>**a.** Some upgrades have the subtype *region*. All *regions* have the text "Limit 1 *region* per server."
    - <a id="rule_region_one_root"></a>**b.** The *region* limitation applies only to cards with the subtype *region* that are installed or in the process of being installed in the root of a server. It does not apply to cards in central servers, and it does not apply to ice.
    - <a id="rule_region_active_inactive"></a>**c.** The *region* limitation applies to all cards as specified in [rule 3.6.5b](#rule_region_one_root), regardless of the active or inactive state of those cards.
    - <a id="rule_region_install"></a>**d.** When the Corp installs a card with the subtype *region* into the root of a server that already has a *region*, they must trash the already-installed *region*. See [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
    - <a id="rule_region_swap"></a>**e.** The Corp cannot use an effect that swaps cards or moves an installed card between servers to put more than 1 *region* into the root of a server.

## <a id="sec_events"></a>3.7. Events

- <a id="rule_event_play"></a>**3.7.1.** Events are the only Runner cards that are played. Events are never installed. Playing an event causes its abilities to become active, and once those abilities are fully resolved the event is trashed. See [section 8.6](#sec_playing_events_and_operations).
    - <a id="rule_event_condition"></a>**a.** One event, On the Lam, has the subtype *condition*. As part of its abilities, it converts itself into a condition counter and installs itself. This event cannot be installed by an install effect; it can only be installed through its own conversion ability. See [rule 10.1.4](#rule_card_to_counter).
    - <a id="rule_event_current"></a>**b.** Some events have the subtype *current*. As part of their abilities, *current* events prevent themselves from being trashed until another *current* operation or event is played or the Corp scores an agenda. When one of these occurs, the active *current* is trashed during the next checkpoint. See [rule 8.6.6c](#rule_play_not_trashed_until).
- <a id="rule_event_play_cost"></a>**3.7.2.** Events are the only Runner cards with play costs. An event's play cost is the number of credits the Runner must spend to play the event. See [section 8.6](#sec_playing_events_and_operations).

## <a id="sec_hardware"></a>3.8. Hardware

- <a id="rule_hardware_piece_of_hardware"></a>**3.8.1.** "Hardware" and "piece of hardware" are synonymous.
- <a id="rule_hardware_install"></a>**3.8.2.** The Runner installs hardware into the play area.
- <a id="rule_hardware_install_cost"></a>**3.8.3.** Hardware are among the types of cards with install costs. The Runner installs a hardware faceup and active by paying its install cost. See [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
- <a id="rule_hardware_no_limit"></a>**3.8.4.** There is no limit to the number of hardware the Runner can have installed.
- <a id="subsec_consoles"></a>**3.8.5.** ***Consoles***
    - <a id="rule_hardware_console"></a>**a.** Some hardware have the subtype *console*. All *consoles* have the text "Limit 1 *console* per player."
    - <a id="rule_install_console"></a>**b.** If a player ever controls more than one installed *console*, all but the most recently active *console* are trashed. Trashing cards this way cannot be prevented.
    - <a id="rule_console_limitation"></a>**c.** This limitation applies only to hardware with the subtype *console*.
    - <a id="rule_console_limitation_active"></a>**d.** This limitation applies only to active *console*. A player can have multiple copies of a *console*, and even different *consoles*, in their deck.

## <a id="sec_programs"></a>3.9. Programs

- <a id="rule_program_install"></a>**3.9.1.** The Runner installs programs into the play area.
- <a id="rule_program_install_cost"></a>**3.9.2.** Programs are among the types of cards with install costs. The Runner installs a program faceup and active by paying its install cost. See [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
- <a id="rule_program_memory_cost"></a>**3.9.3.** Programs are the only card type that have a memory cost. A program's memory cost is the number of memory units that program requires while it is installed.
    - <a id="rule_program_memory"></a>**a.** Installing a program does not permanently consume memory. The program claims its designated quantity of memory units until it becomes uninstalled, at which point those memory units become available for use by other programs.
    - <a id="rule_program_install_exceed_memory_limit"></a>**b.** When the Runner installs a program that would increase the total memory cost of installed programs over their memory limit, they must trash one or more installed programs such that the total memory cost of installed programs including the new program will not exceed their memory limit. See [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
    - <a id="rule_program_other_exceed_memory_limit"></a>**c.** If the Runner's memory limit decreases below the total memory cost of installed programs, or if the total memory cost of installed programs increases above the memory limit for any reason other than installing another program, the Runner must trash one or more installed programs such that the total memory cost of the remaining programs does not exceed their memory limit. This occurs during the next checkpoint after the memory limit is exceeded. See [section 10.3](#sec_checkpoints).
    - <a id="rule_program_memory_not_a_cost"></a>**d.** A program's memory cost is not a cost. It cannot be increased, lowered, ignored, or otherwise modified by abilities that affect costs to install programs.
- <a id="rule_program_strength_value"></a>**3.9.4.** Programs are among the card types that have strength.
    - <a id="rule_icebreakers_strength_value"></a>**a.** Programs with the subtype *icebreaker* have a printed numerical strength value. An *icebreaker*'s strength determines which ice it is allowed to interface with. See [section 3.9.5](#subsec_icebreakers).
    - <a id="rule_programs_without_strength_value"></a>**b.** Programs without the subtype *icebreaker* do not have a strength value. A dash (–) is printed on non-*icebreaker* program cards in the place where strength is printed on *icebreaker* program cards.
- <a id="subsec_icebreakers"></a>**3.9.5.** ***Icebreakers***
    - <a id="rule_program_icebreaker"></a>**a.** Some programs have the subtype *icebreaker*. *Icebreaker* programs have strength and abilities that interface with ice.
    - <a id="rule_icebreaker_strength_increase_implicit"></a>**b.** Paid abilities on an *icebreaker* that modify that *icebreaker*'s strength implicitly have a duration of "for the remainder of the current encounter".
        - > *Example:* Corroder has the ability "1[credit]: +1 strength."" Since no duration is specified, this strength increase applies until the end of the current encounter.
    - <a id="rule_icebreaker_strength_increase_specified"></a>**c.** If an *icebreaker*'s paid ability specifies another duration for modifying its strength, that modification lasts until both the stated duration and the implicit encounter duration have expired.
        - > *Example:* Gordian Blade has the ability "1[credit]: +1 strength for the remainder of this run." This ability applies until the end of the current run, whether it is triggered during an encounter or another step of the run. If the ability is triggered during an encounter that takes place outside of a run, the strength increase applies for the duration of that encounter.
    - <a id="rule_icebreaker_strength_increase_outside_of_encounter"></a>**d.** If an *icebreaker*'s paid ability modifies its strength outside of an encounter and does not specify another applicable duration, the modification expires during the next checkpoint.
        - > *Example:* If the Runner uses Corroder's ability to increase its strength outside of an encounter, it applies only until the next checkpoint.
    - <a id="rule_icebreaker_interface"></a>**e.** All *icebreakers* have abilities that **interface** with ice, sometimes referred to as "interface abilities". These abilities are indicated by the "interface" flag preceding the ability's text.
    - <a id="rule_icebreaker_interface_during_encounter"></a>**f.** The Runner can only use an interface ability during [step 6.9.3b](#step_encounter_paw) of an encounter, and can only choose the encountered piece of ice and its subroutines as targets for the ability's effects. See [section 1.15](#sec_targets) for more information about targeting.
    - <a id="rule_icebreaker_interface_strength"></a>**g.** The Runner can only use an interface ability on an *icebreaker* if the *icebreaker* has strength greater than or equal to the strength of the encountered ice.
    - <a id="rule_icebreaker_interface_subtype"></a>**h.** If an interface ability specifies that it affects a particular subtype of ice, then that ability can only be used on a piece of ice that has the specified subtype. If an ability on an *icebreaker* does not specify a subtype of ice that it affects, that ability can be used on any piece of ice.

## <a id="sec_resources"></a>3.10. Resources

- <a id="rule_resource_install"></a>**3.10.1.** The Runner installs resources into the play area.
- <a id="rule_resource_install_cost"></a>**3.10.2.** Resources are among the types of cards with install costs. The Runner installs a resource faceup and active by paying its install cost. See [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
- <a id="rule_resource_no_limit"></a>**3.10.3.** There is no limit to the number of resources the Runner can have installed.
- <a id="rule_resource_trash_action"></a>**3.10.4.** While the Runner is tagged, the Corp may spend [click] and 2[credit] to trash an installed resource as a basic action. See [section 10.5](#sec_tags).

# <a id="chpt_game_zones"></a>4. Game Zones

## <a id="sec_game_zones_general"></a>4.1. General

- <a id="rule_zone"></a>**4.1.1.** A **zone** is a place in which cards and counters can exist during the game.
    - <a id="rule_zone_types"></a>**a.** There are 8 zone types: deck, hand, discard pile, score area, play area, bank, set aside, and removed from the game.
    - <a id="rule_player_zones"></a>**b.** Each player has their own deck, hand, discard pile, and score area. All players share the play area, the bank, the set-aside zone, and the removed-from-game zone.
    - <a id="rule_card_in_one_zone"></a>**c.** Each card in the game exists in exactly 1 zone at a time.
- <a id="rule_move_between_zones"></a>**4.1.2.** Game rules and card effects can move cards and counters between zones. See [section 8.2](#sec_card_movements) for rules on card movements.
    - <a id="rule_reveal_from_hidden"></a>**a.** If a facedown card or a card in a hidden zone would ever move to another zone as part of an effect that requires that card to have a specific attribute, that card must be revealed before being moved to the new zone. If the card is being installed, this rule only applies as indicated by [section 8.5.13](#rule_install_from_hidden_or_secret_zone).
        - > *Example:* When resolving Clone Suffrage Movement, the Corp chooses a facedown Fast Break in Archives. The Corp must reveal the Fast Break before adding it to HQ, to demonstrate that it is indeed an operation, meeting Clone Suffrage Movement's requirement.
    - <a id="rule_move_instantaneous"></a>**b.** When a card is moved from one zone to another, the move is instantaneous. The card is never in both zones at once, and it is never not in any zone at all.
- <a id="rule_outside_game"></a>**4.1.3.** A card that is not in any zone is outside the game. Cards outside of the game are distinct from cards in the removed-from-game zone, and cannot affect the game. They can only be affected by abilities that explicitly bring in extra cards from outside the game. See [section 1.5](#sec_extra_cards).
- <a id="rule_public_zone"></a>**4.1.4.** A **public** zone is one where cards are freely visible to both players unless they are facedown. The play area, the bank, the set-aside zone, the removed-from-game zone, and both players' score areas and discard piles are public zones. See [section 1.21](#sec_card_visibility) for rules on card visibility and [section 8.1](#sec_faceup_facedown_status) for rules on the faceup and facedown status of a card.
- <a id="rule_hidden_zone"></a>**4.1.5.** A **hidden** zone is one where cards are not visible to either player. Each player's deck is a hidden zone.
- <a id="rule_secret_zone"></a>**4.1.6.** A **secret** zone is one where cards are visible only to their controller. Each player's hand is a secret zone.
- <a id="rule_location"></a>**4.1.7.** A **location** is a specific position within a zone that an object can occupy. A **destination** is a location to which an object is to be moved.
    - <a id="rule_deck_location"></a>**a.** A card in a player's deck has a location identified by how many cards it is from the top of that deck.
    - <a id="rule_play_area_location"></a>**b.** Some objects in the play area are in specific locations. See [section 4.6.5](#rule_play_area_organization).
    - <a id="rule_hosted_object_location"></a>**c.** The location of a hosted object is its host card.
    - <a id="rule_default_location"></a>**d.** All other objects are simply located in their zone and do not have a more specific location.

## <a id="sec_deck"></a>4.2. Deck

- <a id="rule_draw_deck"></a>**4.2.1.** When the game begins, each player's deck presented during setup becomes their in-game draw deck.
- <a id="rule_deck_hidden"></a>**4.2.2.** Decks must be kept facedown with their cards hidden from both players. Neither player may look at the cards in any deck without being explicitly directed to do so by a rule or card ability.
- <a id="rule_deck_ordered"></a>**4.2.3.** Decks are ordered. The location of each card in a deck must be maintained except when a player is explicitly directed to manipulate the cards in a deck by a rule or card ability. See [rule 4.1.7a](#rule_deck_location).
    - <a id="rule_deck_order_while_searching"></a>**a.** While searching a deck, a player is not required to maintain the order of the searched cards or indicate the previous location of any card found by the search. Note that the deck is always shuffled upon completion of the search, as required by [rule 8.7.3](#rule_shuffle_deck_after_search).
- <a id="rule_deck_size_open_info"></a>**4.2.4.** The number of cards in a deck is open information. Any player may count the number of cards in a deck at any time.
- <a id="rule_deck_inactive"></a>**4.2.5.** Cards in a deck are inactive.
- <a id="rule_deck_name"></a>**4.2.6.** Each player's deck has its own name as a means of differentiating between the two.
- <a id="subsec_rnd"></a>**4.2.7.** ***R&D***
    - <a id="rule_rnd_name"></a>**a.** **R&D** is the name for the Corp's deck. It is also one of the Corp's central servers. See [section 4.6.7](#subsec_central_servers).
    - <a id="rule_rnd_empty_rnd"></a>**b.** If the Corp must ever draw from R&D while it is empty, the Runner wins the game. See [section 1.7](#sec_ending_the_game).
- <a id="subsec_stack"></a>**4.2.8.** ***Stack***
    - <a id="rule_stack_name"></a>**a.** The **stack** is the name for the Runner's deck.

## <a id="sec_hand"></a>4.3. Hand

- <a id="rule_hand"></a>**4.3.1.** A player's **hand** is the zone in which they hold the cards they have drawn from their deck. Cards can be added to a player's hand by other rules and card abilities as well. During setup, each player draws a starting hand. See [section 1.6](#sec_starting_the_game).
- <a id="rule_hand_secret"></a>**4.3.2.** A player's hand is kept secret from their opponent. A player may look at the cards in their own hand, but not at any of the cards in their opponent's hands.
- <a id="rule_hand_unordered"></a>**4.3.3.** Hands are not ordered. A player may freely arrange the cards in their hand in any order at any time.
- <a id="rule_hand_size_open_info"></a>**4.3.4.** The number of cards in a hand is open information to all players. Any player may count the number of cards in a hand at any time.
- <a id="rule_hand_inactive"></a>**4.3.5.** Cards in a hand are inactive.
- <a id="rule_maximum_hand_size"></a>**4.3.6.** Each player has a maximum hand size, usually five. A player may have any number of cards in their hand at any given time, but during the discard phase of a player's turn they must discard cards from their hand until there are no more cards in their hand than their maximum hand size allows. See [section 5.5](#sec_discard_phase).
- <a id="rule_hand_name"></a>**4.3.7.** Each player's hand has its own name as a means of differentiating between the two.
- <a id="subsec_hq"></a>**4.3.8.** ***HQ***
    - <a id="rule_hq_name"></a>**a.** **HQ** is the name for the Corp's hand. It is also one of the Corp's central servers. See [section 4.6.7](#subsec_central_servers).
    - <a id="rule_identity_represents_hq"></a>**b.** The Corp's identity card represents their HQ server for the purposes of gameplay. Ice protecting HQ is installed in front of the Corp's identity card and upgrades installed in the root of HQ are installed behind the Corp's identity card.
- <a id="subsec_grip"></a>**4.3.9.** ***Grip***
    - <a id="rule_grip_name"></a>**a.** The **grip** is the name for the Runner's hand.

## <a id="sec_discard_pile"></a>4.4. Discard Pile

- <a id="rule_discard_pile"></a>**4.4.1.** A player's **discard pile** is the zone in which they place cards that have been trashed or discarded. Discard piles start the game empty.
- <a id="rule_discard_pile_unordered"></a>**4.4.2.** Discard piles are not ordered. A player may freely arrange the cards in their discard pile in any order at any time.
- <a id="rule_discard_pile_open_info"></a>**4.4.3.** The number of cards in a discard pile is open information. Any player may count the number of cards in a discard pile at any time.
- <a id="rule_discard_pile_inactive"></a>**4.4.4.** Cards in a discard pile are inactive.
- <a id="rule_discard_pile_inactive_name"></a>**4.4.5.** Each player's discard pile has its own name as a means of differentiating between the two.
- <a id="subsec_archives"></a>**4.4.6.** ***Archives***
    - <a id="rule_archives_name"></a>**a.** **Archives** is the name for the Corp's discard pile. It is also one of the Corp's central servers. See [section 4.6.7](#subsec_central_servers).
    - <a id="rule_archives_faceup_facedown"></a>**b.** Cards in Archives can be either faceup or facedown. If a Corp card is visible to the Runner when it is trashed or discarded, it is put in Archives faceup. If a Corp card is not visible to the Runner when it is trashed or discarded, then it is put in Archives facedown. Facedown cards in Archives should be oriented horizontally so that the Runner can easily see they are present.
    - <a id="rule_archives_faceup_open_info"></a>**c.** The faceup cards in Archives are open information. Any player may look through the faceup cards in Archives at any time. The facedown cards in Archives are visible only to the Corp. The Corp may look through the facedown cards in Archives at any time, but the Runner cannot look at them.
- <a id="subsec_heap"></a>**4.4.7.** ***Heap***
    - <a id="rule_heap_name"></a>**a.** The **heap** is the name for the Runner's discard pile.
    - <a id="rule_heap_open_info"></a>**b.** The cards in the heap are open information. Any player may look through the cards in the heap at any time.

## <a id="sec_score_area"></a>4.5. Score Area

- <a id="rule_score_area"></a>**4.5.1.** A player's **score area** is the zone in which they place agendas they have scored or stolen. [Section 1.17](#sec_score_scoring_steal) details scoring and stealing agendas. Score areas start the game empty.
- <a id="rule_score_area_open_info"></a>**4.5.2.** The agendas in a score area are open information. A player may look through or count the agendas in a score area at any time.
- <a id="rule_score_area_unordered"></a>**4.5.3.** Score areas are not ordered. A player may freely arrange the agendas in their score area in any order at any time.
- <a id="rule_score_area_active_inactive"></a>**4.5.4.** Agendas in the Corp's score area are active. Agendas in the Runner's score area are inactive unless stated otherwise.

## <a id="sec_play_area"></a>4.6. Play Area

- <a id="rule_play_area"></a>**4.6.1.** The **play area** is the shared active zone into which cards are played or installed. The play area normally starts the game empty except for each of the player's identities.
    - <a id="rule_play_area_start_game"></a>**a.** [Section 1.5](#sec_extra_cards) details cases where an ability may cause cards to start the game in the play area.
- <a id="rule_play_area_number_location_open_info"></a>**4.6.2.** The number of cards in the play area and the location of each card in the play area is always open information.
- <a id="rule_play_area_card_open_info"></a>**4.6.3.** Whether or not a card itself and its attributes are open information while in the play area is determined by the card's status. Cards that are faceup are open information and can be examined by any player at any time. Cards that are facedown are secret information and can only be examined by the player who controls the card. See [section 10.2](#sec_information).
- <a id="rule_play_area_active_inactive"></a>**4.6.4.** Whether or not a card is active while in the play area is determined by the card's status.
    - <a id="rule_play_area_identity"></a>**a.** Identities start the game in the play area and are always active.
    - <a id="rule_play_area_corp_cards"></a>**b.** Generally, Corp agendas, assets, ice, and upgrades are installed into the play area unrezzed and thus inactive. They are active while rezzed. See [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
    - <a id="rule_play_area_runner_cards"></a>**c.** Generally, Runner hardware, programs, and resources are installed into the play area faceup and active. See [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
    - <a id="rule_play_area_faceup_facedown"></a>**d.** Some Runner cards are installed facedown or turned facedown through card abilities. This is distinct from the status of rezzed/unrezzed that Corp cards can have. Facedown Runner cards are not active. See [section 8.1.4](#sec_facedown_runner_cards).
    - <a id="rule_play_area_operations_events"></a>**e.** Operations and events are played into the play area faceup and are active for the duration of their resolution before being trashed. See [section 8.6](#sec_playing_events_and_operations).
- <a id="rule_play_area_organization"></a>**4.6.5.** Some objects in the play area are organized into specific orientations and/or locations.
    - <a id="rule_play_area_corp_cards_distinct_server"></a>**a.** Corp cards are arranged into distinct servers. Agendas and assets are installed in the roots of remote servers, upgrades are installed in the roots of any servers, and pieces of ice are installed horizontally protecting servers. See [section 4.6.6](#subsec_servers) and [section 4.6.9](#subsec_protecting_a_server).
    - <a id="rule_play_area_corp_identity"></a>**b.** The Corp's identity is used to indicate where in the play area HQ exists for the purposes of installing upgrades in the root of HQ and ice protecting HQ. See [section 4.6.7](#subsec_central_servers).
    - <a id="rule_play_area_runner_identity"></a>**c.** The Runner's identity, hardware, programs, and resources do not have specific locations in the play area.
    - <a id="rule_play_area_operation_events"></a>**d.** There is no specific location in the play area for operations or events. They are simply played into the play area, where they resolve before being trashed. See [section 8.6](#sec_playing_events_and_operations).
    - <a id="rule_play_area_credit_pool"></a>**e.** Credits in the play area are always placed in a designated location: the Corp's credit pool, the Runner's credit pool, the bad publicity fund, or hosted on a card. See [section 1.10](#sec_credits) for information about credit pools, and see [section 10.6](#sec_bad_publicity) for information about the bad publicity fund.
    - <a id="rule_play_area_counters"></a>**f.** Tag counters and bad publicity counters have no specific location in the play area unless hosted on a card. Other types of counters are either nonfunctional gameplay aids or appear only in host relationships. See also [section 1.9](#sec_counters_and_tokens).
    - <a id="rule_play_area_installed_hosted"></a>**g.** Some cards can act as install locations for other cards while in the play area, such that cards of a specific type can be hosted directly onto them during the installation process. In this type of host relationship, both the host card and the hosted card are in the play area, but the host relationship can affect certain aspects of either the card or the installation process. See [section 1.13](#sec_host).
    - <a id="rule_play_area_not_installed_hosted"></a>**h.** Some cards can host other cards without installing them. In this type of host relationship, both the host card and the hosted card are in the play area, but the hosted card is not installed and thus not active. See [section 1.13](#sec_host).
- <a id="subsec_servers"></a>**4.6.6.** ***Servers***
    - <a id="rule_server"></a>**a.** A **server** is a set of locations where the Corp installs their cards into the play area and against which the Runner can initiate runs. Whenever a Corp installs an agenda, asset, ice, or upgrade, they must choose a destination server for that card. See [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
    - <a id="rule_corp_cards_cannot_be_moved"></a>**b.** Once a Corp card is installed, it cannot be moved from its server without explicit direction from a game rule or card effect.
    - <a id="rule_server_types"></a>**c.** The two types of servers are central servers and remote servers. See sections [4.6.7](#subsec_central_servers) and [4.6.8](#subsec_remote_servers), respectively.
    - <a id="rule_server_ice"></a>**d.** All servers can have ice installed protecting them. See [section 4.6.9](#subsec_protecting_a_server).
    - <a id="rule_server_root"></a>**e.** All servers have a **root** where cards can be installed. A central server's root can contain any number of upgrades, while a remote server's root can contain 1 asset or agenda and any number of upgrades. Assets and agendas cannot be installed in the root of a central server.
    - <a id="rule_server_root_order"></a>**f.** All cards installed in the root of a server are kept in the same orientation and location together. The order in which the cards are installed is open information, but the type of cards installed in the root of a remote server should not be derivable from their orientation or location.
    - <a id="rule_region_unique"></a>**g.** Only 1 *region* upgrade can be installed in the root of a server at a time. See [section 3.6.5](#subsec_regions).
    - <a id="rule_upgrade_server_restriction"></a>**h.** Some upgrades have restrictions limiting them to specific servers. See [rule 8.5.12](#rule_x_server_only).
    - <a id="rule_this_server"></a>**i.** Some cards refer to the server where they are located (or the central server corresponding to the zone where they are located) using the phrase "this server". If an ability is initiated by a trigger condition or cost involving the ability's source card moving from a server, its root, or protecting it to another server or to a location not associated with any server, treat "this server" as referring to the server associated with the previous location of the card throughout resolution of that ability. If an ability moves its source card while it is resolving, continue to interpret "this server" based on the card's previous location.
        - > *Example:* The Runner trashes a rezzed Warroid Tracker. This meets the trigger condition of Warroid Tracker's ability, but by the time a checkpoint is resolved and an instance of the ability would become pending, Warroid Tracker itself is no longer installed. The use of "this server" in the trigger condition still refers to the server from which Warroid Tracker was trashed, not to the server of Warroid Tracker's new location in Archives.
        - > *Example:* The Corp uses the ability granted by ZATO City Grid to trash Border Control and resolve its first subroutine. The phrase "this server" in that subroutine refers to the server from which the Border Control was trashed. However, Border Control is no longer protecting that server, so it is not included in the number of ice counted.
        - > *Example:* The Corp uses Nanisivik Grid to turn a facedown Border Control in Archives faceup and resolve its first subroutine. Border Control was not moved between servers, so "this server" in that subroutine refers to Archives.
    - <a id="rule_another_server"></a>**j.** If an ability on a card currently or previously located in a server, its root, or protecting it refers to "another server", this means any server except "this server" as interpreted by [rule 4.6.6i](#rule_this_server). If a card's abilities involve choosing a server, "another server" in a linked ability means any server except the chosen server. If neither of the previous cases apply and a run is in progress, "another server" means any server except the attacked server.
    - <a id="rule_host_server"></a>**k.** If an ability on an object uses the phrase "this server", and that object is hosted on another object (see [section 1.13](#sec_host)), the server referred to is the server of the host object.
- <a id="subsec_central_servers"></a>**4.6.7.** ***Central Servers***
    - <a id="rule_three_central_servers"></a>**a.** The Corp has three **central servers** at all times. Each central server corresponds to one of the Corp's zones: HQ corresponds to the Corp's hand, R&D corresponds to the Corp's deck, and Archives corresponds to the Corp's discard pile.
    - <a id="rule_hq_identity"></a>**b.** The Corp's identity is in the play area to indicate the location of HQ relative to other servers, but it is not in HQ, in the root of HQ, or protecting HQ. The cards in HQ are held in the Corp's hand. The upgrades installed in the root of HQ are placed in a vertical orientation near the Corp's identity. The ice installed protecting HQ are placed in a horizontal orientation in front of the Corp's identity outwards towards the Runner.
    - <a id="rule_rnd_deck"></a>**c.** The Corp's deck is placed in the play area to indicate the location of R&D relative to other servers, but the cards in the deck are not installed. The cards in R&D are the cards in the deck. The upgrades installed in the root of R&D are placed in a vertical orientation near the deck. The ice installed protecting R&D are placed in a horizontal orientation in front of the deck outwards towards the Runner.
    - <a id="rule_archives_discard_pile"></a>**d.** The Corp's discard pile is placed in the play area to indicate the location of Archives relative to other servers, but the cards in the discard pile are not installed. The cards in Archives are the cards in the discard pile. The upgrades installed in the root of Archives are placed in a vertical orientation near the discard pile. The ice installed protecting Archives are placed in a horizontal orientation in front of the discard pile outwards towards the Runner.
- <a id="subsec_remote_servers"></a>**4.6.8.** ***Remote Servers***
    - <a id="rule_remote_server"></a>**a.** A **remote server** is the only type of server that can contain an asset or agenda in its root.
    - <a id="rule_creating_remote_servers"></a>**b.** The Corp starts the game with no remote servers. The Corp creates a new remote server by declaring it as the destination server for a card they are about to install. There is usually no limit to the number of remote servers the Corp can have at any given time.
    - <a id="rule_separate_remote_servers"></a>**c.** Cards installed in the roots of separate remote servers are placed in a vertical orientation in a row extending away from the central servers to indicate the locations of the remote servers relative to other servers. The ice installed protecting each remote server are placed in a horizontal orientation in front of any cards in the root of that server, outwards towards the Runner.
    - <a id="rule_remote_server_existence"></a>**d.** A remote server exists as long as there is at least one card in the root of or protecting it. A remote server can be made up of only ice protecting it, only cards installed in it, or a combination of both.
    - <a id="rule_remote_server_cease_to_exist"></a>**e.** During checkpoints, a remote server ceases to exist if there are no cards installed in its root and no cards installed protecting it. If a server ceases to exist during a run against it, then the run ends after any currently open paid ability window closes. Unless the run has already been declared either successful or unsuccessful, it is neither. See [rule 8.5.8](#rule_server_ceases_to_exist).
    - <a id="rule_limit_remote_servers"></a>**f.** Some abilities limit the number of remote servers. While such an ability is active, the Corp cannot create a new remote server if this would raise the total number of remote servers above the specified limit. During [step 10.3.1e](#step_checkpoint_card_restrictions) of each checkpoint, if there are more remote servers than an active limit allows, the Corp player chooses a number of servers equal to the limit. The checkpoint then trashes all cards in the root of and protecting each remote server that was not chosen.
        - > *Example:* The Corp's identity is Earth Station: SEA Headquarters, which has the ability "Limit 1 remote server." If any remote servers exist, the Corp cannot create a new remote server.
- <a id="subsec_protecting_a_server"></a>**4.6.9.** ***Protecting a Server***
    - <a id="rule_ice_ordered"></a>**a.** An installed piece of ice is arranged in a horizontal position in front of the server it is protecting. Ice are ordered within the play area. The location of a piece of ice is defined by the server it is protecting and the number of other pieces of ice between it and the server.
    - <a id="rule_innermost_ice"></a>**b.** If there is no other ice between the server and a piece of ice, that ice is the innermost piece of ice. If there is no ice farther from the server than a piece of ice, that ice is the outermost piece of ice. If there is only one piece of ice protecting a server, that ice is both the innermost and the outermost piece of ice.
    - <a id="rule_ice_same_position"></a>**c.** If two pieces of ice protecting two different servers share the same number of ice between them and their respective servers, those pieces of ice are considered to be in the same position.
    - <a id="rule_ice_install_outermost"></a>**d.** When the Corp installs a piece of ice protecting a server, they must place it in the outermost position protecting that server. See [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
    - <a id="rule_ice_move"></a>**e.** If the number of other ice between a piece of ice and the server changes, its position will change accordingly, moving either closer or farther from the server. If this occurs while that ice is being approached, encountered, or passed during a run, the Runner's position attacking the server changes in the same way.

## <a id="sec_bank"></a>4.7. Bank

- <a id="rule_bank_counter_supply"></a>**4.7.1.** The **bank** is an inactive zone shared by both players. It holds an unlimited supply of counters for use during gameplay.
- <a id="rule_counters_default_from_bank"></a>**4.7.2.** Counters placed or loaded onto a card are taken from the bank unless otherwise specified. Counters gained by players are taken from the bank unless otherwise specified.
- <a id="rule_spent_counters_returned_to_bank"></a>**4.7.3.** Credits and other counters that are spent to pay costs return to the bank.
- <a id="rule_illegal_location_counters_returned_to_bank"></a>**4.7.4.** Counters that are not in a legal location return to the bank during checkpoints. See [section 10.3](#sec_checkpoints).
- <a id="rule_cards_never_in_bank"></a>**4.7.5.** Cards are never located in the bank.

## <a id="sec_set_aside"></a>4.8. Set Aside

- <a id="rule_set_aside_zone"></a>**4.8.1.** The **set-aside zone** is an inactive zone shared by both players. It is used as a temporary holding space for objects being affected by abilities.
- <a id="rule_set_aside"></a>**4.8.2.** If an object is "set aside", it is moved from its current zone to the set-aside zone.
- <a id="rule_set_aside_zone_passthrough"></a>**4.8.3.** An ability cannot see the set-aside zone unless it specifically sets objects aside or refers to set-aside objects. Outside of these cases, cards moved into another zone from the set-aside zone are treated as though they entered that zone directly from their location prior to being set aside.
    - > *Example:* The Runner is playing as Exile and plays Test Run, searching their heap for a program. Although the program is set aside before being installed, Exile's ability treats it as though it were installed directly from the heap, and so Exile will draw a card.
- <a id="rule_searched_cards_set_aside"></a>**4.8.4.** Cards found during a search are set aside facedown while the search completes (in particular, while any deck that was searched is shuffled). See [section 8.7](#sec_searching_cards).
- <a id="rule_set_aside_hosted_counters_trash_ability"></a>**4.8.5.** Some paid abilities with a trigger cost that uninstalls or forfeits the ability's source card need to refer to or act on that card's hosted cards or counters. These abilities set aside the hosted cards or counters as the trigger cost is paid. See [rule 9.5.5](#rule_trash_ability_keeps_track_of_hosted_objects).
- <a id="rule_set_aside_default_faceup"></a>**4.8.6.** Cards set aside by a card ability are faceup unless the ability specifically instructs that they be set aside facedown.
- <a id="rule_facedown_set_aside_distinct_groups"></a>**4.8.7.** Facedown cards in the set-aside zone must be kept in distinct groups according to the effect that sets them aside. Cards within such a group are not ordered and can be freely arranged by their controller.
    - > *Example:* The Runner accesses the top card of R&D and does not steal or trash it. On the next turn, the Corp uses Daily Business Show to draw that card and 1 other card simultaneously, but must put 1 of the drawn cards on the bottom of R&D. While resolving the draw, the cards are set aside facedown, so the Corp can shuffle them. They do not have to tell the Runner whether the previously-accessed card ends up in HQ or on the bottom of R&D.

## <a id="sec_removed_from_game"></a>4.9. Remove from the Game

- <a id="rule_removed_from_game_zone"></a>**4.9.1.** The **removed-from-game zone** is an inactive zone shared by both players. It is used to take cards out of any player's control until the end of the game.
- <a id="rule_rfg_move"></a>**4.9.2.** If a card is removed from the game, it is moved from its current zone to the removed-from-game zone.
- <a id="rule_forfeit_rfg"></a>**4.9.3.** Forfeiting an agenda removes it from the game. See [rule 8.2.5](#movement_forfeit).
- <a id="rule_rfg_inactive"></a>**4.9.4.** Cards that have been removed from the game are inactive.
- <a id="rule_rfg_no_interaction"></a>**4.9.5.** A card that has been removed from the game cannot move out of the removed-from-game zone or otherwise be interacted with.
- <a id="rule_rfg_open_information"></a>**4.9.6.** Cards that have been removed from the game are open information. Any player may look through or count the cards that have been removed from the game at any time.

# <a id="chpt_turns"></a>5. Turns

## <a id="sec_turn_general"></a>5.1. General

- <a id="rule_turn"></a>**5.1.1.** A **turn** is a duration of time during which a player may take actions and is the first to receive priority to act during priority windows.
- <a id="rule_corp_turn_phases"></a>**5.1.2.** The Corp's turn consists of three phases. During the **draw phase**, the Corp draws the top card of R&D into HQ; during the **action phase**, the Corp spends [click]s to perform actions; during the **discard phase**, the Corp discards until the number of cards in HQ does not exceed their maximum hand size. Once the Corp's turn ends, the Runner's turn begins.
- <a id="rule_runner_turn_phases"></a>**5.1.3.** The Runner's turn consists of two phases. During the **action phase**, the Runner spends [click]s to perform actions; during the **discard phase**, the Runner discards until the number of cards in their grip does not exceed their maximum hand size. Once the Runner's turn ends, the Corp's turn begins.
- <a id="rule_begin_end_turn_step"></a>**5.1.4.** The beginning and end of each player's turn are formalized as particular steps of the turn timing structures.
    - <a id="rule_turn_begin_trigger_conditions"></a>**a.** Trigger conditions related to a turn beginning are not met as the turn timing structure opens. They are met at the timing step that indicates the formal beginning of the turn.
    - <a id="rule_turn_end_trigger_conditions"></a>**b.** Trigger conditions related to a turn or discard phase ending are met at the timing step that indicates the formal end of the turn. They are not met when the turn timing structure later closes. The end of the turn and the end of the discard phase are the same.
    - <a id="rule_before_turn_begins"></a>**c.** Some effects can occur before the "turn begins" step or after the "turn ends" step of a turn. The player whose turn timing structure is in progress is still the active player, and effects that apply during their turn are still applicable, even though their turn has not yet formally begun or has already formally ended.
    - <a id="rule_never_between_turns"></a>**d.** Once the game begins, it is always one or the other player's turn. There is no time "between turns".

## <a id="sec_actions"></a>5.2. Actions

- <a id="rule_action_definition"></a>**5.2.1.** An **action** is any paid ability where the cost begins with a [click] symbol.
    - <a id="rule_costs_with_click"></a>**a.** Other costs can contain [click] symbols without denoting an action.
        - > *Example:* The ability "[click]: Gain 1[credit] and draw 1 card." on Professional Contacts is an action. The ability "Lose [click]: Break 1 subroutine on this ice. Only the Runner can use this ability." on Eli 1.0 is not an action and is used during a paid ability window.
- <a id="rule_initiate_action"></a>**5.2.2.** A player initiates an action by paying its cost in [click], as well as any other costs associated with that action, during their action window.
    - <a id="rule_action_completion"></a>**a.** Once an action is initiated, it must be completed before the game can advance to the next step or open another action window. If the effects of an action initiate a timing structure, such as a run, players may use paid abilities, rez cards, and score agendas as dictated by the priority windows of that nested timing structure, but otherwise players cannot perform any of those voluntary effects while the action is resolving. See [section 9.2](#sec_timing_and_priority) for more details about priority.
    - <a id="rule_action_timing_structure_completion"></a>**b.** If a timing structure is initiated during the resolution of an action, that action is not complete until the new timing structure is complete and any further effects of the initiated action following the completion of the new timing structure are resolved.
        - > *Example:* The Runner takes the action "play an event", playing Stimhack. The action is not complete until the run initiated by Stimhack ends, the Runner suffers core damage, and Stimhack is trashed following its resolution.
    - <a id="rule_action_conditional_ability_trigger"></a>**c.** A conditional ability that meets its trigger condition due to a player taking an action resolves after the action's trigger cost is paid, before the effects of the action itself. See [rule 9.1.2a](#rule_chain_reaction).
    - <a id="rule_finish_action_trigger_condition"></a>**d.** Some conditional abilities meet their trigger conditions due to an action "ending", "finishing", or "completing". These abilities resolve in the reaction window following [step 5.6.2b](#step_corp_turn_action) or [step 5.7.1f](#step_runner_turn_action), for actions taken by the Corp and Runner, respectively.
- <a id="rule_basic_actions"></a>**5.2.3.** Each player has several basic actions they can always perform. The Corp's basic actions are listed in [section 5.2.6](#rule_corp_actions), and the Runner's basic actions are listed in [section 5.2.7](#rule_runner_actions).
    - <a id="rule_with_the_basic_action"></a>**a.** If an ability has a trigger condition related to an effect being performed “with the basic action”, that trigger condition is met by any basic action that performs the indicated effect. If an ability imposes a cost on performing an effect “with the basic action”, that cost applies to any instance of the indicated effect that would occur as part of resolving a basic action.
- <a id="rule_actions_outside_action_phase"></a>**5.2.4.** A player can only take actions during the action phase of their turn. If an effect directs a player to take an action outside of that player's action phase, instead that player does nothing.
- <a id="rule_same_different_actions"></a>**5.2.5.** Some cards refer to players taking "the same action" or "different actions".
    - <a id="rule_same_actions"></a>**a.** Actions a player performs are the same if they are all the same basic action or if all of those actions were initiated by the same ability from the same card.
    - <a id="rule_defferent_actions"></a>**b.** Actions a player performs are different if each of them is initiated from a different basic action or card ability. Instances of equivalent abilities on different cards are still different actions.
        - > *Example:* The Corp, playing MirrorMorph: Endless Iteration, plays Hedge Fund as the first action of their turn, then plays Lateral Growth. Even though two different cards were played, both times the Corp used the basic action to "Play 1 operation from HQ". Since their first two actions were the same, the Corp will not be able to trigger the ability on MirrorMorph this turn.
- <a id="rule_corp_actions"></a>**5.2.6.** Corp Actions
    - <a id="rule_corp_basic_actions"></a>**a.** The following **basic actions** are always available to the Corp during their action phase, in addition to any actions provided by card abilities.
    - <a id="rule_corp_basic_action_credit"></a>**b.** "[click]: Gain 1[credit]."
    - <a id="rule_corp_basic_action_draw"></a>**c.** "[click]: Draw 1 card."
    - <a id="rule_corp_basic_action_install"></a>**d.** "[click]: Install 1 agenda, asset, upgrade, or piece of ice from HQ." See [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
    - <a id="rule_corp_basic_action_operation"></a>**e.** "[click]: Play 1 operation from HQ." See [section 8.6](#sec_playing_events_and_operations).
    - <a id="corp_basic_action_advance"></a>**f.** "[click], 1[credit]: Advance 1 installed card." See [section 1.18](#sec_advancing_cards).
    - <a id="corp_basic_action_trash_resource"></a>**g.** "[click], 2[credit]: Trash 1 resource. Take this action only if the Runner is tagged." See [section 10.5](#sec_tags).
    - <a id="corp_basic_action_purge_virus_counters"></a>**h.** "[click][click][click]: Purge virus counters." See [rule 10.1.2](#rule_purge).
- <a id="rule_runner_actions"></a>**5.2.7.** Runner Actions
    - <a id="rule_runner_basic_actions"></a>**a.** The following **basic actions** are always available to the Runner during their action phase, in addition to any actions provided by card abilities.
    - <a id="runner_basic_action_credit"></a>**b.** "[click]: Gain 1[credit]."
    - <a id="runner_basic_action_card"></a>**c.** "[click]: Draw 1 card."
    - <a id="runner_basic_action_install"></a>**d.** "[click]: Install 1 program, resource, or piece of hardware from the grip." See [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
    - <a id="runner_basic_action_event"></a>**e.** "[click]: Play 1 event from the grip." See [section 8.6](#sec_playing_events_and_operations).
    - <a id="runner_basic_action_run"></a>**f.** "[click]: Run any server." See [section 6](#chpt_runs).
    - <a id="runner_basic_action_remove_tag"></a>**g.** "[click], 2[credit]: Remove 1 tag." See [section 10.5](#sec_tags).

## <a id="sec_draw_phase"></a>5.3. Draw Phase

- <a id="rule_draw_phase_definition"></a>**5.3.1.** The draw phase is the first phase of the Corp's turn. It includes the formal beginning of the Corp's turn and the Corp's mandatory draw.
- <a id="rule_mandatory_draw"></a>**5.3.2.** The **mandatory draw** is a card draw performed by the Corp. It is not an action and does not cost [click].
- <a id="rule_no_runner_draw_phase"></a>**5.3.3.** The Runner's turn does not have a draw phase.

## <a id="sec_action_phase"></a>5.4. Action Phase

- <a id="rule_action_phase_definition"></a>**5.4.1.** The action phase is the first phase of the Runner's turn and the second phase of the Corp's turn. It is the only timing structure in which players can take actions, as discussed in [section 5.2](#sec_actions).
- <a id="rule_action_phase_duration"></a>**5.4.2.** During a player's action phase, they must continue to take actions until they have no more clicks or the phase is ended directly by a card ability. The player may take actions in any combination and order as long as they pay the appropriate costs. Once they have no remaining clicks, the action phase ends.
- <a id="rule_forced_action_phase_end"></a>**5.4.3.** A player's action phase can be forced to end by certain card abilities (e.g. via the ability on a *terminal* operation or event). When a player's action phase is forced to end, the following occurs:
    - <a id="rule_terminal_step_ends"></a>**a.** The current step of the action phase ends, and all remaining steps are skipped.
    - <a id="rule_terminal_priority_windows_close"></a>**b.** Any currently open priority windows close, their associated abilities lose their pending status, and the player with priority loses priority.
    - <a id="rule_terminal_completion"></a>**c.** Any currently resolving actions or abilities cease resolving and are considered complete.
    - <a id="rule_terminal_checkpoint"></a>**d.** Conditions related to the action phase ending are met, and a checkpoint occurs. A reaction window created by this checkpoint is still considered to be taking place in the action phase, but players cannot take actions during this window.
    - <a id="rule_terminal_to_discard_phase"></a>**e.** After completing any reaction windows opened by the checkpoint from [rule 5.4.3d](#rule_terminal_checkpoint), the game proceeds to the active player's discard phase.
- <a id="rule_end_action_phase_outside_action_phase"></a>**5.4.4.** If an effect attempts to end a player's action phase during their draw phase, their discard phase, or another player's turn, this effect does nothing.

## <a id="sec_discard_phase"></a>5.5. Discard Phase

- <a id="rule_discard_phase_definition"></a>**5.5.1.** The discard phase is the last phase of each player's turn. It contains the discard step and the formal end of that player's turn.
