- <a id="rule_discard_phase_discarding"></a>**5.5.2.** **discarding** is the act by which a player moves a card to their discard pile during their discard phase if they have exceeded their maximum hand size.
    - <a id="rule_discarding_facedown_status"></a>**a.** Cards discarded from HQ are always sent to Archives facedown, regardless of whether they have been previously accessed by the Runner.
    - <a id="rule_discarding_trashed"></a>**b.** A discarded card is not considered to have been trashed, and vice versa.
- <a id="rule_max_hand_size"></a>**5.5.3.** A player's **maximum hand size** is the maximum number of cards that player can keep in their hand during their discard phase.
    - <a id="rule_max_hand_size_default"></a>**a.** Players begin the game with a maximum hand size of five cards.
    - <a id="rule_max_hand_size_core_damage"></a>**b.** Each point of core damage the Runner suffers permanently reduces their maximum hand size by 1 card. See [section 10.4](#sec_damage). Other abilities can modify a player's maximum hand size directly.
- <a id="rule_discard_step"></a>**5.5.4.** The **discard step** is [step 5.6.3a](#step_corp_turn_discard) for the Corp, and [step 5.7.2a](#step_runner_turn_discard) for the Runner. In that step, the active player discards cards to reach their maximum hand size.
    - <a id="rule_runner_discard_step"></a>**a.** Before the Runner discards cards in their discard step, they must check for a flatline. See [rule 1.7.2b](#rule_flatline).
    - <a id="rule_discard_step_noop"></a>**b.** If the number of cards in the active player's hand is less than or equal to their maximum hand size, nothing else happens in this step.
    - <a id="rule_discarding_process"></a>**c.** If the number of cards in the active player's hand is greater than their maximum hand size, they discard a number of cards from their hand equal to the difference. The discarding player chooses which of their cards to discard, and the chosen cards are all discarded simultaneously.
    - <a id="rule_skip_discard_step"></a>**d.** If an effect instructs a player to skip their discard step, the game proceeds directly from the start of their discard phase to the step following the discard step. They will not check their hand size or discard cards.

## <a id="sec_steps_corp_turn"></a>5.6. Steps of the Corp's Turn

- <a id="subsec_corp_draw_phase"></a>**5.6.1.** During the draw phase, the following steps occur in order:
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_allotted_clicks"></a>**a.** The Corp gains their allotted clicks (default [click][click][click]).
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_draw_phase_paw"></a>**b.** A paid ability window occurs, in which players may use paid abilities, the Corp may rez non-ice cards, and the Corp may score agendas. (P) (R) (S)
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_recurring_credits_refill"></a>**c.** The Corp's recurring credits ([recurring]) refill.
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_turn_formal_begin"></a>**d.** The Corp's turn formally begins. Conditions related to the turn beginning are met.
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_mandatory_draw"></a>**e.** The Corp performs their mandatory draw.
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_draw_phase_complete"></a>**f.** The Corp's draw phase is complete. Play proceeds to the Corp's action phase.
- <a id="subsec_corp_action_phase"></a>**5.6.2.** During the Corp's action phase, the following steps occur in order:
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_action_phase_paw"></a>**a.** A paid ability window occurs, in which players may use paid abilities, the Corp may rez non-ice cards, and the Corp may score agendas. (P) (R) (S)
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_action"></a>**b.** If the Corp has any unspent [click], the Corp takes an action. Otherwise, skip to (d).
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_action_phase_loop"></a>**c.** Return to (a).
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_action_phase_end"></a>**d.** The Corp's action phase formally ends. Conditions related to the action phase ending are met.
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_action_phase_complete"></a>**e.** The Corp's action phase is complete. Play proceeds to the Corp's discard phase.
- <a id="subsec_corp_discard_phase"></a>**5.6.3.** During the Corp's discard phase, the following steps occur in order:
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_discard"></a>**a.** The Corp discards cards to reach their maximum hand size.
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_discard_phase_paw"></a>**b.** A paid ability window occurs, in which players may use paid abilities and the Corp may rez non-ice cards. (P) (R)
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_lose_unspent_clicks"></a>**c.** If the Corp has any unspent [click], the Corp loses those [click].
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_formal_end"></a>**d.** The Corp's turn formally ends. Conditions related to the turn or discard phase ending are met.
    - <a id="step_corp_turn_complete"></a>**e.** The Corp's discard phase and turn are complete. Play proceeds to the Runner's turn.

## <a id="sec_steps_runner_turn"></a>5.7. Steps of the Runner's Turn

- <a id="subsec_runner_action_phase"></a>**5.7.1.** During the Runner's action phase, the following steps occur in order:
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_allotted_clicks"></a>**a.** The Runner gains allotted clicks (default [click][click][click][click]).
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_action_phase_paw"></a>**b.** A paid ability window occurs, in which players may use paid abilities and the Corp may rez non-ice cards. (P) (R)
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_recurring_credits_refill"></a>**c.** The Runner's recurring credits ([recurring]) refill.
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_recurring_formal_begin"></a>**d.** The Runner's turn formally begins. Conditions related to the turn beginning are met.
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_loop_paw"></a>**e.** A paid ability window occurs, in which players may use paid abilities and the Corp may rez non-ice cards. (P) (R)
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_action"></a>**f.** If the Runner has any unspent [click], the Runner takes an action. Otherwise, skip to (h).
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_action_loop"></a>**g.** Return to (e).
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_action_phase_end"></a>**h.** The Runner's action phase formally ends. Conditions related to the action phase ending are met.
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_action_phase_complete"></a>**i.** The Runner's action phase is complete. Play proceeds to the Runner's discard phase.
- <a id="subsec_runner_discard_phase"></a>**5.7.2.** During the Runner's discard phase, the following steps occur in order:
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_discard"></a>**a.** The Runner discards cards to reach their maximum hand size.
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_discard_phase_paw"></a>**b.** A paid ability window occurs, in which players may use paid abilities and the Corp may rez non-ice cards. (P) (R)
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_lose_unspent_clicks"></a>**c.** If the Runner has any unspent [click], the Runner loses those [click].
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_formal_end"></a>**d.** The Runner's turn formally ends. Conditions related to the turn or discard phase ending are met.
    - <a id="step_runner_turn_complete"></a>**e.** The Runner's discard phase and turn are complete. Play proceeds to the Corp's turn.

# <a id="chpt_runs"></a>6. Runs

## <a id="sec_run_general"></a>6.1. General

- <a id="rule_run"></a>**6.1.1.** A **run** is a Runner's attack on a server. There are 6 phases used in the procedure of carrying out a run, described in sections [6.3](#sec_run_initiation) through [6.8](#sec_run_ends_phase). A run always begins with the Initiation Phase. [Section 6.9](#sec_steps_of_a_run) contains the full set of steps carried out during each phase, and should be referred to in conjunction with the sections discussing the phases.
- <a id="rule_attacked_server"></a>**6.1.2.** The **attacked server** is the server the Runner is attempting to reach, usually to breach that server.
    - <a id="rule_announce_attacked_server"></a>**a.** The Runner announces the attacked server in the initiation phase of the run.
    - <a id="rule_change_attacked_server"></a>**b.** Card abilities can sometimes change the attacked server during the run. This does not end the run, but it can affect whether or not certain card abilities or effects apply.
    - <a id="rule_change_runner_position"></a>**c.** If an ability changes the Runner's position, the attacked server and the current timing step of the run could also change. See [section 6.2.8](#rule_move_runner_to_position).
    - <a id="rule_change_attacked_server_directly"></a>**d.** A few abilities change the attacked server directly, without referring to the Runner's position. These abilities do not change the current timing step of the run.
        - > *Example:* Sneakdoor Beta's ability changes the attacked server from Archives to HQ. The Runner does not need to encounter ice protecting HQ after resolving this ability.
- <a id="rule_modify_run_steps"></a>**6.1.3.** Many abilities modify the sequence of steps taken to resolve a run. Such abilities can direct players to skip steps or phases, replace steps with other effects, move directly from the current timing point to a new phase, or make other changes to the procedures explained in this section. Heed the Golden Rules in [section 1.2](#sec_golden_rules).
    - <a id="rule_directly_approach_encounter"></a>**a.** Some abilities direct the Runner to approach or encounter a piece of ice as part of moving them to that ice's position. See [section 6.2.8](#rule_move_runner_to_position).
    - <a id="rule_approach_directly"></a>**b.** One card, Code Replicator, directs the Runner to approach the piece of ice in their position without first moving them to a new position. This effect changes the current timing step of the run to [step 6.9.2](#rule_sec_run_approach_ice_phase), the Approach Ice Phase.
    - <a id="rule_forced_encounter_link"></a>**c.** An ability that directs the Runner to encounter a piece of ice without first moving the Runner's position does not change the current timing point of the run, the attacked server, or the Runner's position. The effect of such an ability is to resolve an Encounter Ice Phase independent of the current state of the run. See [section 6.5.9](#subsec_forced_encounters).
    - <a id="rule_move_new_run_phase"></a>**d.** Any time the current timing step of the run is moved to a different phase of the run, except for an instruction to end the run (See [section 6.1.4](#rule_end_the_run), below), no more steps in the previously-active phase are carried out. If a paid ability window is open, players complete that window normally. Then the active phase is complete and a checkpoint occurs. Finally, the game proceeds to the new phase.
    - <a id="rule_run_phase_after"></a>**e.** If a condition refers to encountering a piece of ice "after" an approach or passing a piece of ice "after" an encounter, then that condition is only met by the indicated phases or steps occurring in direct sequence according to the standard progression of the run.
        - > *Example:* The Runner uses Inversificator to break the subroutine on a Mirāju that is protecting Archives. When the encounter ends, Mirāju's ability creates a replacement effect so that the Runner moves to the outermost position of Archives instead of passing Mirāju. Because the ice is not passed, Inversificator's ability does not meet its trigger condition. Later, the Runner encounters the same Mirāju again and does not break its subroutine. When this encounter ends, the Runner passes Mirāju. Even though the Runner has used Inversificator to fully break Mirāju during an encounter earlier in the run, Inversificator's trigger condition is not met when the Runner passes Mirāju because the Runner is not passing it after the relevant encounter.
    - <a id="rule_pass_after_breaking"></a>**f.** If a condition refers to passing a piece of ice "after" fully breaking it, then that condition is only met when the Runner passes that piece of ice after an encounter (as defined by [rule 6.1.3e](#rule_run_phase_after)), and only if the Runner fully broke that ice during that encounter. See [section 6.5.7](#subsec_fully_break).
- <a id="rule_end_the_run"></a>**6.1.4.** To **end the run** is to halt the Runner's progress toward the server. When an effect ends the run, the current phase of the run ends without following any of its remaining steps, and before completing open priority windows. The game immediately moves from the current timing point to [step 6.9.6](#rule_sec_run_run_ends_phase), the Run Ends Phase.
    - <a id="rule_end_run_access_or_breach_during_run"></a>**a.** If a run is in progress, an instruction to end the run also ends any breach and/or access taking place.
    - <a id="rule_end_encounter_outside_run"></a>**b.** If an instruction to end the run resolves while there is no run in progress, but there is an encounter in progress, that encounter ends without resolving any more of its steps. Process any open priority windows in the same way as described in [section 6.8.2](#rule_run_ends_process_priority_windows), but do not resolve any other steps of the Run Ends Phase. Any breach and/or access initiated during the encounter also ends, but a breach and/or access that was already in progress when the encounter began is not ended.
        - > *Example:* The Runner uses Detente to access a card in HQ while there is no run in progress. They access Ganked! and are forced to encounter Loot Box. The Runner chooses to neither break any subroutines nor spend credits for Loot Box's first subroutine, so that subroutine tries to end the run. There is no run to end, but this effect still ends the encounter. The second subroutine on Loot Box will not resolve.
    - <a id="rule_end_run_no_run_or_encounter"></a>**c.** If an instruction to end the run resolves while there is neither a run nor an encounter in progress, that effect does nothing.
        - > *Example:* The Corp plays Unleash and resolves the last subroutine on Lycian Multi-Munition. The Corp will gain 1[credit], but there is no further effect.
- <a id="rule_jacking_out"></a>**6.1.5.** **Jacking out** is the process by which a Runner voluntarily ends a run. Jacking out follows the usual process for ending the run as described in [section 6.1.4](#rule_end_the_run), but some card abilities function differently depending on whether or not the Runner chose to end the run by jacking out.
    - <a id="rule_jack_out_after_passing_ice"></a>**a.** The Runner has the opportunity to jack out after passing a piece of ice.
    - <a id="rule_jack_out_before_approach"></a>**b.** If there is no ice protecting the server, the Runner has the opportunity to jack out before approaching the server.

## <a id="sec_position"></a>6.2. Position

- <a id="rule_position"></a>**6.2.1.** Each server has a sequence of **positions** that assign an order to ice protecting that server. A piece of ice protecting a server always occupies exactly 1 position in that server's sequence at a time.
    - <a id="rule_hosted_ice_has_no_position"></a>**a.** A piece of ice that is hosted on another card is not protecting a server and does not occupy a position, regardless of whether the ice is installed.
- <a id="rule_create_position"></a>**6.2.2.** When a piece of ice is installed protecting a server or an installed piece of ice is moved, a new position is created for that ice. For ice being installed, the position is created when the Corp declares an install destination for the ice ([step 8.5.16b](#rule_steps_installing_destination)), and the ice occupies that position once the ice becomes installed ([step 8.5.16e](#rule_steps_installing_become_installed)). For installed ice being moved, the position is created when the movement happens, and the ice occupies that position immediately.
    - <a id="rule_create_position_outermost"></a>**a.** If a piece of ice is being installed in or moved to the outermost position protecting a server, the new position is outward from the outermost already-existing position. If an instruction to install a piece of ice does not explicitly specify otherwise, the ice is installed in the outermost position.
    - <a id="rule_create_position_innermost"></a>**b.** If a piece of ice is being installed in or moved to the innermost position protecting a server, the new position is inward from the innermost already-existing position.
    - <a id="rule_create_position_directly_inward"></a>**c.** If a piece of ice is being installed "directly inward" from another piece of ice, the new position is inward from that ice's position, and outward from the next already-existing position inward, if there is one.
    - <a id="rule_create_position_any_position"></a>**d.** If a piece of ice is being installed "in any position", the new position can be created outward from all existing positions, inward from all existing positions, or between any two existing consecutive positions, as the Corp chooses.
    - <a id="rule_create_position_mutate"></a>**e.** One card, Mutate, has an ability that installs a piece of ice in the "same position" as a particular piece of ice that was previously trashed. This is a different usage of "same position" from that described in [rule 6.2.3](#rule_count_positions). For this installation, the ice is installed protecting the same server the trashed ice was protecting, and the new position is created at the same place in that server's sequence of positions as the position the trashed ice had.
    - <a id="rule_create_position_swap"></a>**f.** If a piece of ice is swapped with a piece of ice protecting a server, no new position is created. The first ice will occupy the position previously held by the second ice (and vice-versa, if the first piece of ice was also protecting a server).
- <a id="rule_count_positions"></a>**6.2.3.** Some abilities refer to ice in the "same position" protecting different servers. Two pieces of ice occupy the "same position" if their respective servers have the same number of positions inward from their respective positions.
    - > *Example:* A server has 2 pieces of ice protecting it, and a copy of Rook is hosted on the outermost of those 2 pieces of ice. Rook's ability can move it to the 2nd piece of ice protecting any server, counted starting from the innermost piece of ice and moving outward. The Rook cannot be moved to a server protected by only 1 piece of ice.
    - > *Example:* The Runner passes a piece of ice in the innermost position protecting a server, and uses Slipstream to move to another server. Since positions are compared working from the innermost position, the Runner will be able to approach the innermost ice protecting any server, regardless of how many other pieces of ice are outward from that ice.
- <a id="rule_destroy_position"></a>**6.2.4.** If a piece of ice leaves its position, except by being swapped with another piece of ice, its former position ceases to exist. This happens during [step 10.3.1i](#step_checkpoint_vacant_position) of the next checkpoint, unless the Runner is in that position, in which case the position continues to exist until the Runner moves to another position or ceases to have a position.
- <a id="rule_position_runner"></a>**6.2.5.** During a run, the Runner occupies the attacked server's positions.
    - <a id="rule_position_initial"></a>**a.** The Runner enters the outermost position protecting the attacked server at [step 6.9.1d](#step_runner_position) of the Initiation Phase.
    - <a id="rule_position_progression"></a>**b.** The Runner progresses through the positions of the attacked server in order. While the Runner occupies a position, they approach, encounter (if the ice is rezzed), and pass the ice in that position, then they proceed to the next position moving inward, until they complete this process for the innermost position or the run is ended.
    - <a id="rule_no_position_after_innermost_ice"></a>**c.** Once the Runner leaves the innermost position protecting a server, they no longer have a position. This occurs at [step 6.9.4d](#step_move_position) of the Movement Phase. However, abilities can still move the Runner to a position later in that phase.
    - <a id="rule_no_position_after_approach_server"></a>**d.** During the Success Phase and the Run Ends Phase, the Runner has no position and can no longer move to a position.
        - > *Example:* The Corp uses Ganked! to force the Runner to encounter Cell Portal in the middle of breaching a server. Since the run is already successful, resolving Cell Portal's subroutine will have no meaningful effect except to derez Cell Portal.
    - <a id="rule_no_position_outside_run"></a>**e.** The Runner does not have a position while they are not engaged in a run, nor do they maintain their position from one run to the next.
- <a id="rule_ice_change_can_affect_position"></a>**6.2.6.** The Runner's current position is a specific element of the sequence of positions, not an index into that sequence. Adding or removing other positions protecting the attacked server does not affect the Runner's position.
    - <a id="rule_ice_change_outward"></a>**a.** If a position outward from the Runner's current position is added to the attacked server, the Runner will normally not need to approach or encounter the new piece of ice to reach the server.
        - > *Example:* The Runner runs a server protected by 2 pieces of ice. The Runner approaches the outermost ice, and the Corp rezzes Drafter. During the subsequent encounter, the Corp resolves Drafter's second subroutine, installing a new piece of ice protecting the attacked server and trashing both of the other pieces of ice in the process. The new ice is installed to the outermost position protecting the server, which is outward from the Runner's current position, so the Runner will not approach the new piece of ice. This is true even though the number of positions between the new ice and the server is less than the number of positions that were previously between the Runner and the server.
    - <a id="rule_ice_change_inward"></a>**b.** If a position inward from the Runner's current position is added to or removed from the attacked server, the ice the Runner will need to approach and/or encounter changes accordingly.
        - > *Example:* The Runner encounters Brân 1.0 and breaks its last two subroutines. Brân 1.0's first subroutine resolves and the Corp installs a new piece of ice in the next inward position protecting the server. The Runner will have to approach that new piece of ice later in this run because its position is inward from their current position.
- <a id="rule_ice_change_current_position"></a>**6.2.7.** Changes to the piece of ice in the Runner's current position can affect the progression of the run, but they do so differently during an encounter than outside of an encounter.
    - <a id="rule_ice_change_approach_uninstall_move"></a>**a.** If a piece of ice is uninstalled or moved to another position (protecting this or another server) while it is being approached, the approach ends and the run continues to the Movement Phase.
    - <a id="rule_ice_change_approach_swap"></a>**b.** If a piece of ice currently being approached is swapped with another piece of ice, the Runner remains in their current position and the run remains in the Approach Ice Phase. The Runner is now approaching the new piece of ice occupying that position. Trigger conditions related to a piece of ice being approached are not met for the new ice.
    - <a id="rule_ice_change_encounter_uninstall_derez"></a>**c.** If a piece of ice is uninstalled or derezzed while it is being encountered, the Encounter Phase ends and the run continues to the Movement Phase.
    - <a id="rule_ice_change_encounter_move_swap"></a>**d.** If a piece of ice currently being encountered moves to another position or is swapped with installed ice in another position, the Runner remains with the encountered ice and continues the run from that ice's new position.
        - > *Example:* The Runner encounters Bullfrog as the innermost piece of ice protecting a remote server. Bullfrog's subroutine resolves and it is moved to be the outermost piece of ice protecting Archives. The Runner is now running on Archives and continues the run from Bullfrog's new position.
    - <a id="rule_ice_change_during_movement"></a>**e.** If the piece of ice in the Runner's position is uninstalled, derezzed, moved, or swapped during the Initiation Phase or the Movement Phase, the Runner is not moved and the current timing step of the run does not change.
        - > *Example:* A run begins, and before the Runner approaches the outermost piece of ice protecting the attacked server, the Corp uses Project Yagi-Uda to swap that ice with another piece of ice. The Runner will approach the new ice.
        - > *Example:* The Runner passes a piece of ice, and in [step 6.9.4b](#step_before_jack_out_paw), the Corp uses Project Yagi-Uda to swap that ice with another piece of ice. The Runner has still passed their position (but has not passed the new ice occupying that position) and will proceed to the next position in [step 6.9.4d](#step_move_position). They will not approach or encounter the new ice.
- <a id="rule_move_runner_to_position"></a>**6.2.8.** During a run, abilities can move the Runner directly to a position.
    - <a id="rule_move_to_piece_of_ice"></a>**a.** If an ability instructs the Runner to move to a piece of ice in a different position, the Runner's position becomes the position corresponding to that ice, and the server that ice protects becomes the attacked server. The current timing step becomes [step 6.9.2](#rule_sec_run_approach_ice_phase), the Approach Ice Phase, or [step 6.9.3](#rule_sec_run_encounter_ice_phase), the Encounter Ice Phase, according to whether the Runner was directed to approach or encounter the ice.
    - <a id="rule_move_to_specific_position"></a>**b.** If an ability instructs the Runner to move to "the outermost position" of a server, that server becomes the attacked server. If there is any ice protecting that server, the Runner's position becomes the position of the outermost such piece of ice, and the current timing step becomes [step 6.9.2](#rule_sec_run_approach_ice_phase), the Approach Ice Phase. If there is no ice, the Runner ceases to have a position, and the current timing step becomes [step 6.9.4](#rule_sec_run_movement_phase), the Movement Phase.
    - <a id="rule_ineffective_move"></a>**c.** If an ability instructs the Runner to move to a position during the Success Phase, during the Run Ends Phase, or while there is no run in progress, instead the Runner does nothing. See [rule 6.2.5d](#rule_no_position_after_approach_server).
    - <a id="rule_move_to_current_ice"></a>**d.** If an ability instructs the Runner to move to and approach a position they are already approaching, instead the Runner does nothing.

## <a id="sec_run_initiation"></a>6.3. Initiation

- <a id="rule_run_initiation_phase"></a>**6.3.1.** The **initiation phase** sets the parameters for the run and begins the run.
- <a id="rule_start_initiation_phase"></a>**6.3.2.** At the start of the Initiation Phase, the Runner announces a server to be the attacked server.
    - <a id="rule_cannot_run_abilities"></a>**a.** Some abilities state that the Runner "cannot run on" or "cannot initiate a run on" certain servers under certain conditions. These effects refer to the announcement of the attacked server in the Initiation Phase.
        - > *Example:* Off the Grid states in part, "The Runner cannot initiate a run on this server." This only prohibits the Runner from announcing this particular remote server as the attacked server at the beginning of the run. If an ability changes the attacked server while a run is in progress, Off the Grid does not interfere with that ability.
    - <a id="rule_additional_cost_to_run"></a>**b.** Some abilities impose an additional cost to run, either generally or on a particular server. Any costs to run a server must be paid at the time that server is announced as the attacked server.
- <a id="rule_initiation_bad_publicity"></a>**6.3.3.** After the attacked server is announced, credits are added to the Runner's bad publicity fund. See [section 10.6](#sec_bad_publicity) for an explanation of bad publicity.
- <a id="rule_abilities_during_a_run"></a>**6.3.4.** Some abilities look for particular conditions occurring "during a run" or are restricted to being used "during a run." For purposes of abilities, the run formally begins only after the attacked server is announced and any costs are paid.
    - > *Example:* The Runner initiates a run against a server with a rezzed Heinlein Grid while the Corp has Enhanced Login Protocol in play. They do not lose any credits to Heinlein Grid's ability, as the additional click Enhanced Login Protocol requires them to spend is spent to initiate the run, and is not spent during the run.
- <a id="rule_run_initiation_phase_next_phase"></a>**6.3.5.** When the Initiation Phase completes, the run moves either to the Approach Ice Phase ([section 6.4](#sec_approach_ice_phase)) or the Movement Phase ([section 6.6](#sec_movement_phase)). If there is ice protecting the attacked server, the Runner's position becomes the outermost piece of ice, and the run proceeds to the Approach Ice Phase. If there is no ice protecting the server, then the run moves directly to the Movement Phase.

## <a id="sec_approach_ice_phase"></a>6.4. Approach Ice

- <a id="rule_approach_ice_phase"></a>**6.4.1.** The **approach ice phase** gives the Corp the opportunity to rez the ice in the Runner's current position.
- <a id="rule_approach_condition"></a>**6.4.2.** For purposes of meeting trigger conditions, the Runner approaching a piece of ice is equivalent to the Approach Ice Phase beginning at that ice's position. Abilities with this trigger condition are subject to [rule 9.2.8f](#rule_reaction_window_closing_timing_structure).
- <a id="rule_rez_ice_restriction"></a>**6.4.3.** The paid ability window in [step 6.9.2b](#step_approach_paw) of the Approach Ice Phase is the only time the Corp can normally rez ice. In this window, the Corp only gains the ability to rez the piece of ice being approached, not any other ice.
- <a id="rule_approach_ice_next_phase"></a>**6.4.4.** When the Approach Ice Phase completes, the run moves either to the Encounter Ice Phase ([section 6.5](#sec_encounter_ice_phase)) or the Movement Phase ([section 6.6](#sec_movement_phase)). If the approached piece of ice is rezzed, the Runner encounters it next. If the approached piece of ice is unrezzed or if there is no longer ice installed in that position, then the run moves directly to the Movement Phase.

## <a id="sec_encounter_ice_phase"></a>6.5. Encounter Ice

- <a id="rule_encounter_ice_phase"></a>**6.5.1.** The **encounter ice phase** gives the Runner the opportunity to interact with the ice in their current position, then requires the Corp to resolve unbroken subroutines on that ice.
- <a id="rule_encounter_condition"></a>**6.5.2.** For purposes of meeting trigger conditions, the Runner encountering a piece of ice is equivalent to the Encounter Ice Phase beginning at that ice's position. Abilities with this trigger condition are subject to [rule 9.2.8f](#rule_reaction_window_closing_timing_structure).
- <a id="rule_encounter_unbroken_sub_status"></a>**6.5.3.** As each encounter begins, each subroutine on the encountered ice gains an "unbroken" status associated with that encounter. See [section 9.8](#sec_subroutines) for details.
- <a id="rule_encounter_break_paw"></a>**6.5.4.** During the paid ability window in the Encounter Ice Phase ([step 6.9.3b](#step_encounter_paw)), the Runner can break subroutines on the ice. See [rule 9.8.5](#rule_break_subroutine).
- <a id="rule_encounter_sub_resolve"></a>**6.5.5.** After the paid ability window in the Encounter Ice Phase closes, unbroken subroutines on the encountered ice resolve. See [section 9.8.7](#rule_resolve_unbroken_subroutines).
- <a id="rule_encounter_ice_next_phase"></a>**6.5.6.** At the end of the Encounter Ice Phase, the run continues to the Movement Phase, [section 6.6](#sec_movement_phase).
- <a id="subsec_fully_break"></a>**6.5.7.** ***Fully Break***
    - <a id="rule_fully_break"></a>**a.** During each encounter, the Runner **fully breaks** the encountered ice the first time all subroutines on that ice are broken.
    - <a id="rule_object_fully_break"></a>**b.** Whenever the Runner fully breaks a piece of ice, if all its subroutines were broken using abilities on a single object, that object also **fully breaks** the ice.
    - <a id="rule_fully_break_no_subroutines"></a>**c.** If an encountered piece of ice has no subroutines, the Runner fully breaks it when [step 6.9.3b](#step_encounter_paw) of the encounter begins. No objects fully break the ice in this case.
    - <a id="rule_fully_broken_not_retracted"></a>**d.** If the encountered piece of ice gains subroutines after the Runner fully breaks it, the Runner's status of having fully broken the ice remains in place. Conditions that check whether the Runner fully broke the ice will still be met as normal. Breaking the new subroutines will not fully break the ice or meet related conditions again.
- <a id="subsec_bypass"></a>**6.5.8.** ***Bypass***
    - <a id="rule_bypass"></a>**a.** When an effect allows the Runner to **bypass** a piece of ice, the Encounter Ice Phase is aborted and the Runner immediately proceeds to pass that ice.
    - <a id="rule_bypass_start_of_encounter"></a>**b.** Most bypass effects occur at the beginning of an encounter and result in steps [6.9.3b](#step_encounter_paw) and [6.9.3c](#step_resolve_subroutine) never occurring, meaning that subroutines on the encountered ice are neither broken nor resolved.
    - <a id="rule_bypass_during_encounter"></a>**c.** A few bypass effects can be triggered later. If ice is bypassed in [step 6.9.3b](#step_encounter_paw), subroutines may or may not have been broken, but [step 6.9.3c](#step_resolve_subroutine) never occurs so no subroutines resolve. Note that for ice with zero subroutines, the Runner has "broken all subroutines" if and only if [step 6.9.3b](#step_encounter_paw) was reached. See [rule 9.12.2d](#rule_vacuous_truth).
        - > *Example:* The Runner plays Forked and encounters a Troll that they can bypass with Femme Fatale. The Runner pays 0[credit] to bypass the Troll with Femme Fatale. Forked does not meet its trigger condition, and Troll is not trashed, because the opportunity to break subroutines was never reached.
- <a id="subsec_forced_encounters"></a>**6.5.9.** ***Forced Encounters***
    - <a id="rule_forced_encounter"></a>**a.** If an ability directs the Runner to encounter a piece of ice without first changing their position, resolve an Encounter Ice Phase but do not change the Runner's position, if any. After the encounter, return to the effect that caused the encounter and proceed from there. This type of encounter outside the normal progression of a run is referred to as a **forced encounter**.
        - > *Example:* If Shiro causes Chrysalis to be accessed, resolve the encounter with Chrysalis and then return to resolving subroutines on Shiro.
    - <a id="rule_forced_encounter_during_run"></a>**b.** During a forced encounter, an instruction to end the run is applied to both the Encounter Ice Phase being resolved and the phase from which the additional encounter was initiated. See also [rule 6.1.4b](#rule_end_encounter_outside_run).
        - > *Example:* The Corp uses The Twins to force the Runner to encounter a piece of ice again when they pass it. If a subroutine directs the Corp to end the run during the extra encounter, both that encounter and the original Movement Phase are aborted, and the game proceeds to the Run Ends phase.
    - <a id="rule_forced_encounter_end"></a>**c.** An instruction that creates a forced encounter is not finished resolving until the encounter is complete.
    - <a id="rule_move_during_forced"></a>**d.** During a forced encounter, if an ability moves the Runner or directs the Runner to approach a piece of ice, that movement resolves and changes the parameters of the underlying run normally, but does not end or interfere with the forced encounter.

## <a id="sec_movement_phase"></a>6.6. Movement

- <a id="rule_movement_phase"></a>**6.6.1.** The **movement phase** handles the progression of the run between pieces of ice and after the innermost piece of ice, up to the point when the Runner approaches the attacked server.
- <a id="rule_pass_ice"></a>**6.6.2.** If the Movement Phase was reached from the Approach Ice Phase or the Encounter Ice Phase, the Runner passes the ice in their position.
- <a id="rule_jack_out_movement_phase"></a>**6.6.3.** During each Movement Phase, the Runner has the opportunity to jack out. See [section 6.1.5](#rule_jacking_out).
- <a id="rule_runner_continues"></a>**6.6.4.** If the Runner declines to jack out, their position moves to the next inward position if there is one.
- <a id="rule_runner_approach_new_position"></a>**6.6.5.** If the Runner moves to a new position, the run continues from the Movement Phase to the Approach Ice Phase, [section 6.4](#sec_approach_ice_phase). The Runner will approach the piece of ice in their new position.
- <a id="rule_approach_server"></a>**6.6.6.** If the Runner does not jack out or return to the Approach Ice Phase, they approach the server. This has no inherent effects, but is referred to by some card abilities. After the Runner approaches the server, the run continues to the Success Phase, [section 6.7](#sec_success_phase).

## <a id="sec_success_phase"></a>6.7. Success

- <a id="rule_success_phase"></a>**6.7.1.** The **success phase** declares the run successful and allows the Runner to breach the attacked server.
- <a id="rule_successful_run"></a>**6.7.2.** A run during which the runner breaks through the Corp's defenses and reaches the server itself is declared a **successful run**. This label is frequently referred to by card abilities.
- <a id="rule_declaring_successful_run"></a>**6.7.3.** Declaring a run to be successful is not the same as the Success Phase beginning. Accordingly, abilities that have trigger conditions related to a successful run are not subject to [rule 9.2.8f](#rule_reaction_window_closing_timing_structure).
- <a id="rule_if_successful"></a>**6.7.4.** Many abilities that initiate a run contain a conditional ability with the trigger condition "If successful". This means, "After the run created this way becomes successful".
    - <a id="rule_if_successful_tied_to_server"></a>**a.** If an effect that initiates a run specifies that that run must be made against a specific server or servers, any "If successful" abilities associated with that effect are tied to that server or servers. If the attacked server has changed since the run was initiated, and no longer is a server that could have been chosen for this run, the "If successful" abilities do not meet their trigger conditions.
        - > *Example:* The Runner plays Because I Can, which directs them to make a run on a remote server. If an effect moves the run to a central server, the "If successful" effect on Because I Can will not apply. However, if the run is moved instead to another remote server, the "If successful" effect is still applied, since Because I Can would have allowed a run on the second remote server to begin with.
    - <a id="rule_if_successful_lingering_effect"></a>**b.** "If successful" abilities frequently create lingering effects (see [section 9.10](#sec_lingering_effects)) that modify the procedure for accessing cards or replace breaching the attacked server with another effect entirely. These effects apply to the breach permitted by the rules for this run (see [rule 6.7.5](#rule_breach_link)), and they expire when the run is complete.
    - <a id="rule_if_successful_ability_optional"></a>**c.** If an "If successful" effect gives the Runner an effect they can optionally carry out instead of breaching, that decision is made at the time the breach would begin, in [step 6.9.5b](#step_breach).
        - > *Example:* The Runner plays Account Siphon while Ash 2X3ZB9CY is installed in the root of HQ, and the run is successful. The Runner can wait until after the trace from Ash 2X3ZB9CY completes before deciding to access cards normally or force the Corp to lose credits.
- <a id="rule_breach_link"></a>**6.7.5.** When the Runner breaches the server, they follow the procedures detailed in [section 7](#chpt_access_breach).

## <a id="sec_run_ends_phase"></a>6.8. Run Ends Phase

- <a id="rule_run_ends_phase"></a>**6.8.1.** The **run ends phase** formalizes the end of the run and prepares the game to return to the timing structure from which the run was initiated.
- <a id="rule_run_ends_process_priority_windows"></a>**6.8.2.** At the beginning of the Run Ends Phase, the game processes each priority window that was open when the run was ended. This is done according to the following procedure:
    - <a id="rule_run_ends_close_paws"></a>**a.** If a paid ability window was open when the run was ended, the window closes. No player can trigger further paid abilities or resolve other options the window would normally allow (such as rezzing cards).
        - > *Example:* The Corp spends a counter from a scored Nisei MK II to end the run. Once this happens, the Runner does not have an opportunity to use their bad publicity credits from this run to pay for using Self-modifying Code.
    - <a id="rule_run_ends_close_reaction_window"></a>**b.** If the run was ended during a reaction window opened due to a phase beginning, the reaction window closes, as per [rule 9.2.8f](#rule_reaction_window_closing_timing_structure). No other pending abilities are resolved.
        - > *Example:* The Runner uses the ability on Security Nexus during a reaction window at the beginning of the Encounter Ice Phase. The trace is successful, so the ability ends the run. If Tollbooth's ability is also pending in this reaction window, it will not be resolved, since the encounter has ended.
    - <a id="rule_run_ends_other_priority_windows"></a>**c.** Any other open priority window is completed normally, except that new timing structures (including a breach that was delayed according to [rule 7.3.8](#rule_consecutive_breaches)) cannot be initiated. If more than one priority window needs to be completed this way, they are resolved in the usual order described in [section 9.1.2](#rule_resolve_ability).
        - > *Example:* The run is ended during the reaction window following [step 6.9.4g](#step_approach_server), while Formicary's ability is still pending. The Corp can rez Formicary and move it to the approached server, but the Runner's position cannot be moved and an encounter cannot be initiated.
- <a id="rule_run_ends_lose_bad_pub_credits"></a>**6.8.3.** During the Run Ends Phase, if there are any credits remaining in the Runner's bad publicity fund, those credits are returned to the bank.
- <a id="rule_unsuccessful_run"></a>**6.8.4.** As part of the Run Ends Phase, the game determines whether the run should be declared an **unsuccessful run**. This is a label applied to runs in which the Runner failed to reach the server they attacked, and is referred to by card abilities. The run is declared unsuccessful if neither of the following conditions have been met:
    - <a id="rule_not_unsuccessful_when_reached_success_phase"></a>**a.** If the run reached [step 6.9.5](#rule_sec_run_success_phase), the Success Phase, it is not declared unsuccessful. This is the case regardless of whether the run was actually declared successful.
        - > *Example:* The Runner reaches a server with Crisium Grid rezzed. Crisium Grid's ability stops the run from being declared successful, but the run is not declared unsuccessful either.
    - <a id="rule_server_cease_to_exist_unsuccesful_exception"></a>**b.** If the attacked server has ceased to exist, the run is not declared unsuccessful. See [rule 8.5.8](#rule_server_ceases_to_exist).
- <a id="rule_run_ends_condition"></a>**6.8.5.** Conditions related to a run ending are not met as soon as an "end the run" effect resolves or at the beginning of the Run Ends Phase. Instead, these conditions are met at [step 6.9.6d](#step_run_complete), when the entire run timing structure is complete. Similarly, static abilities that apply during a run and durations that apply until the end of a run expire at [step 6.9.6d](#step_run_complete).
    - > *Example:* The Runner plays The Noble Path to make a run in which they prevent all damage. During the run, the subroutine on Chum resolves, and then the Runner encounters Wall of Static and does not break its subroutine. When the "end the run" ability resolves, the encounter also ends, so the trigger condition on Chum's delayed conditional ability is met. The ability will resolve and fail to damage the Runner during the Run Ends Phase, because The Noble Path's effect still applies. Similarly, Georgia Emelyov's first ability meets its trigger condition at [step 6.9.6c](#step_run_declared_unsuccessful), which is still before the run is over, so the damage will be prevented.
    - > *Example:* The Runner plays The Noble Path to make a run in which they prevent all damage. The run is successful but the Runner takes a tag during the run. When the run ends, Dedicated Response Team's ability meets its trigger condition at the same time that The Noble Path's effect expires, so the Runner will suffer 2 meat damage.

## <a id="sec_steps_of_a_run"></a>6.9. Steps of a Run

- <a id="rule_sec_run_initiation_phase"></a>**6.9.1.** Initiation Phase
    - <a id="step_initiation_announce"></a>**a.** The Runner announces the attacked server.
    - <a id="step_initiation_bad_publicity"></a>**b.** The Runner adds 1[credit] from the bank to their bad publicity fund for each bad publicity the Corp has.
    - <a id="step_initiation_formal_begin"></a>**c.** The run formally begins. Conditions related to the run beginning or initiating are met.
    - <a id="step_runner_position"></a>**d.** The Runner's position is set corresponding to the outermost ice protecting the attacked server, if any.
    - <a id="step_initiation_paw"></a>**e.** A paid ability window occurs, in which players may use paid abilities and the Corp may rez non-ice cards. (P) (R)
    - <a id="step_initiation_complete"></a>**f.** The Initiation Phase is complete. If the Runner has a position corresponding to a piece of ice, proceed to [step 6.9.2](#rule_sec_run_approach_ice_phase), Approach Ice Phase, approaching the piece of ice corresponding to their position. If the Runner has no position or if there is no ice in their position, proceed to [step 6.9.4](#rule_sec_run_movement_phase), Movement Phase.
- <a id="rule_sec_run_approach_ice_phase"></a>**6.9.2.** Approach Ice Phase
    - <a id="step_approach_begins"></a>**a.** The approach begins. Conditions related to the Runner approaching this ice are met.
    - <a id="step_approach_paw"></a>**b.** A paid ability window occurs, in which players may use paid abilities and the Corp may rez non-ice cards. Additionally, the Corp can rez the approached piece of ice. (P) (R)
    - <a id="step_approach_complete"></a>**c.** The Approach Ice Phase is complete. If the approached piece of ice is rezzed, continue to [step 6.9.3](#rule_sec_run_encounter_ice_phase), Encounter Ice Phase. Otherwise, proceed to [step 6.9.4](#rule_sec_run_movement_phase), Movement Phase.
- <a id="rule_sec_run_encounter_ice_phase"></a>**6.9.3.** Encounter Ice Phase
    - <a id="step_encounter_begins"></a>**a.** The encounter begins. Conditions related to the Runner encountering this ice are met.
    - <a id="step_encounter_paw"></a>**b.** A paid ability window occurs, in which players may only use paid abilities. Subroutines on the encountered ice can be broken during this window. (P)
    - <a id="step_resolve_subroutine"></a>**c.** If there are unbroken subroutines that have not yet resolved this encounter, the Corp resolves the next such subroutine in order. Otherwise, skip to (e).
    - <a id="step_resolve_subroutine_loop"></a>**d.** Return to (c).
    - <a id="step_encounter_complete"></a>**e.** The Encounter Ice Phase is complete. Continue to [step 6.9.4](#rule_sec_run_movement_phase), Movement Phase.
- <a id="rule_sec_run_movement_phase"></a>**6.9.4.** Movement Phase
    - <a id="step_pass_ice"></a>**a.** If the Runner's position corresponds to a piece of ice, the Runner passes that ice. Conditions related to passing ice are met. If there are no positions more inward than the ice just passed, conditions related to passing all of the ice protecting a server are also met.
    - <a id="step_before_jack_out_paw"></a>**b.** A paid ability window occurs, in which players may only use paid abilities. (P)
    - <a id="step_jack_out_choice"></a>**c.** The Runner may choose to jack out, proceeding to [step 6.9.6](#rule_sec_run_run_ends_phase), Run Ends Phase. If the Runner does not, continue to (d).
    - <a id="step_move_position"></a>**d.** The Runner's position moves to the next position inward, if any.
    - <a id="step_after_jack_out_paw"></a>**e.** A paid ability window occurs, in which players may use paid abilities and the Corp may rez non-ice cards. (P) (R)
    - <a id="step_approach_new_ice"></a>**f.** If the Runner moved to a new position, return to [step 6.9.2](#rule_sec_run_approach_ice_phase), Approach Ice Phase, approaching the piece of ice in their new position. If not, continue to (g).
    - <a id="step_approach_server"></a>**g.** The Runner approaches the attacked server. Conditions related to the Runner approaching a server are met.
    - <a id="step_movement_complete"></a>**h.** The Movement Phase is complete. Continue to [step 6.9.5](#rule_sec_run_success_phase), Success Phase.
- <a id="rule_sec_run_success_phase"></a>**6.9.5.** Success Phase
    - <a id="step_run_declared_successful"></a>**a.** The run is declared successful. Conditions related to a successful run are met.
    - <a id="step_breach"></a>**b.** The Runner breaches the attacked server.
    - <a id="step_success_complete"></a>**c.** The Success Phase is complete. Continue to [step 6.9.6](#rule_sec_run_run_ends_phase), Run Ends Phase.
- <a id="rule_sec_run_run_ends_phase"></a>**6.9.6.** Run Ends Phase
    - <a id="step_open_priority_windows_closed"></a>**a.** Any priority windows that were open when the run moved to this phase are completed or closed as described in [section 6.8.2](#rule_run_ends_process_priority_windows).
    - <a id="step_run_ends_bad_publicity"></a>**b.** The Runner returns any remaining credits in the bad publicity fund to the bank.
    - <a id="step_run_declared_unsuccessful"></a>**c.** If the Success Phase was not reached during this run and the attacked server still exists, the run becomes unsuccessful.
    - <a id="step_run_complete"></a>**d.** The Run Ends Phase and the run as a whole are both complete.

# <a id="chpt_access_breach"></a>7. Accessing Cards and Breaching Servers

## <a id="sec_accessing_cards"></a>7.1. Accessing Cards

- <a id="rule_accessing"></a>**7.1.1.** **Accessing** is the process of the Corp showing one of their cards to the Runner for potential interaction.
- <a id="rule_accessing_who_can_look"></a>**7.1.2.** While the Runner is accessing a card, the Runner is allowed to look at that card, even if it would normally not be visible to them. While the Runner is accessing a card, the Corp is allowed to look at it as well, except when the card is being accessed from R&D.
- <a id="rule_accessing_trigger_condition"></a>**7.1.3.** An ability that meets its trigger condition when its source card is accessed is active even if the source card is inactive. See [section 9.1.8](#rule_ability_active_inactive_source_card).
- <a id="rule_mid_access_ability_opportunity"></a>**7.1.4.** During each access, the Runner has 1 opportunity to use a mid-access ability. This occurs after the reaction window at the beginning of the access, and before the Runner steals an accessed agenda.
- <a id="rule_basic_trash_ability"></a>**7.1.5.** The Runner always has the ability "Access → Pay the trash cost of the accessed card: Trash it." This is the **basic trash ability**.
    - <a id="rule_paying_trash_costs"></a>**a.** All assets and upgrades have trash costs, as do some ice and operations. If a card does not have a trash cost, the Runner cannot pay its trash cost, and therefore the Runner cannot use the basic trash ability during that access.
    - <a id="rule_trash_in_archives"></a>**b.** The Runner cannot trash or pay the trash cost of a card in the Corp's discard pile, either with the basic trash ability or with other mid-access abilities.
    - <a id="rule_access_installed_must_trash_if_able"></a>**c.** One card, Mumbad Virtual Tour, has the ability "If the Runner accesses this upgrade while it is installed, they must trash it, if able." When this ability applies, the Runner can use any mid-access ability they have available that trashes the card, but they cannot decline to use a mid-access ability or use one that would not trash the accessed card unless they are unable to pay the cost of any ability that would trash it.
    - <a id="rule_access_reveal_trash_if_able"></a>**d.** One card, Neutralize All Threats, has the ability "The first time each turn you access a card with a trash cost, reveal it. You must trash that card by paying its trash cost, if able." When this ability applies, the Runner can only use the basic trash ability. They cannot decline to use a mid-access ability or use a mid-access ability available from a card unless they cannot pay the cost of the basic trash ability.
- <a id="rule_after_mid_access_agenda"></a>**7.1.6.** If, after resolving mid-access abilities, the Runner is still accessing an agenda, they must steal it.
    - <a id="rule_agenda_additional_cost"></a>**a.** Some abilities can create additional costs to steal an agenda. The Runner can decline to pay such costs and not steal the agenda. See [section 1.16.10](#rule_additional_cost).
- <a id="rule_moved_while_accessing"></a>**7.1.7.** If a card moves to another location while it is being accessed, the access ends immediately.
- <a id="rule_end_run_ends_access"></a>**7.1.8.** An "end the run" effect that resolves while a card is being accessed can sometimes end that access. See [section 6.1.4](#rule_end_the_run).
- <a id="rule_access_set_of_cards"></a>**7.1.9.** One card, Information Sifting, uses a method other than a breach to establish a set of cards for the Runner to access. The Runner accesses those cards one at a time in the order of their choice. Each access is performed as a separate instruction. See [rule 9.11.4b](#rule_split_up_instruction). Accessing cards in this way is an exception to [rule 9.12.2a](#rule_act_on_multiple_cards).
- <a id="rule_access_cards_without_breach"></a>**7.1.10.** One card, Counter Surveillance, instructs the Runner to access a certain number of cards from a server without mentioning a breach. The procedure for these accesses follows the normal rules for breaching that server except: there is no random access limit if the server is HQ or R&D; and the procedure ends once the designated number of cards have been chosen for access.

## <a id="sec_steps_accessing_card"></a>7.2. Steps of Accessing a Card

- <a id="step_card_accessed"></a>**7.2.1.** The card is accessed. Conditions related to accessing this card are met.
- <a id="step_mid_access_ability"></a>**7.2.2.** The Runner may use a single mid-access ability, such as the basic trash ability.
- <a id="step_access_agenda"></a>**7.2.3.** If the accessed card is an agenda, the Runner must steal it.
- <a id="step_access_complete"></a>**7.2.4.** The access is complete. Conditions related to an access ending are met.

## <a id="sec_breaching_servers"></a>7.3. Breaching Servers

- <a id="rule_breaching_servers"></a>**7.3.1.** **Breaching** a server is the process of the Runner accessing a particular set of cards associated with that server. Most accesses occur during a breach. [Step 6.9.5b](#step_breach) of a run calls for the Runner to breach the attacked server, but card abilities can also directly instruct the Runner to breach a server.
    - <a id="rule_visibility_after_access"></a>**a.** After the Runner accesses a card during a breach, that object remains visible to the Runner for the remainder of the breach.
        - > *Example:* The Runner breaches HQ and accesses Scatter Field, Palisade, and Offworld Office, in that order. When the Runner steals Offworld Office, the Corp uses Poétrï Luxury Brands to install a card from HQ. The Runner will know whether the Corp installs the Scatter Field, the Palisade, or an unknown card from the rest of HQ.
- <a id="rule_breaching_archives"></a>**7.3.2.** When the Runner breaches Archives, all the facedown cards in the Corp's discard pile are turned faceup before the Runner accesses any cards.
    - <a id="rule_archives_facedowns_after_breach"></a>**a.** If a card is added to Archives facedown after this step, that card remains facedown for the remainder of the breach, even after it is accessed. The Runner can only look at a facedown card in Archives once they have accessed it.
- <a id="rule_candidates"></a>**7.3.3.** The cards associated with a breached server that the Runner could potentially access are called **Candidates**. The set of candidates is compiled from the cards in the root of the breached server and, for central servers, the cards in the zone that corresponds to that server (see [rule 4.6.7a](#rule_three_central_servers)). [Section 7.4](#sec_determining_candidates) defines which of these cards are candidates.
- <a id="rule_candidates_presented"></a>**7.3.4.** During a breach, the Runner is presented with the current candidates. They choose 1 of those cards and access it. This process repeats until there are no longer any candidates.
    - <a id="rule_candidates_breaching_hq"></a>**a.** When presented with candidates during a breach of HQ, the Runner chooses either a specific candidate that is not in the Corp's hand or to access a random candidate from among the ones in the Corp's hand. A card randomly chosen this way is treated as though the Runner had chosen it.
    - <a id="rule_corp_choosing_candidates_breaching_hq"></a>**b.** One card, Dedicated Neural Net, instructs the Corp to choose which cards the Runner accesses from HQ. While this effect applies, the Runner still chooses candidates that are not in the Corp's hand as normal. Whenever the Runner chooses to access a candidate from the Corp's hand, instead of a candidate being chosen at random, the Corp chooses 1 of those candidates for the Runner to access. The chosen card is still treated as though the Runner had chosen it.
- <a id="rule_random_access_limit"></a>**7.3.5.** The Runner can only choose candidates from the Corp's hand or deck a limited number of times during a breach of HQ or R&D, respectively. This number is called the **Random Access Limit**. Before the Runner begins accessing cards during a breach of HQ or R&D, the random access limit for that breach is determined, after which it will not change for the remainder of that breach.
    - <a id="rule_default_random_access_limit"></a>**a.** By default, the random access limit is 1.
    - <a id="rule_increasing_random_access_limit"></a>**b.** If an ability allows the Runner to "access additional cards" during a breach of HQ or R&D, the effect of that ability is to increase the random access limit. Such an ability can only be applied at the beginning of the breach, before the value of the random access limit is set.
    - <a id="rule_counting_random_access_limit"></a>**c.** If the Runner chooses a candidate in HQ or R&D but does not actually access that card for any reason, that candidate still counts towards reaching the random access limit.
- <a id="rule_number_of_accesses"></a>**7.3.6.** Some abilities count accesses in order to establish a quantity. An ability that counts in this way only includes accesses that are actually performed.
    - > *Example:* The Runner uses Hades Shard to breach Archives during a run on HQ. For 1 of the accesses in that breach, after choosing a candidate, they use Archives Interface to apply a replacement effect, removing the chosen candidate from the game instead of accessing it. When the run ends, the Runner uses Zahya Sadeghi: Versatile Smuggler to gain 1[credit] for each time they accessed a card during the run. The access that was replaced does not contribute to the number of credits the Runner gains.
    - > *Example:* The Runner plays Immolation Script, and the subroutine on Hudson 1.0 resolves before Archives is breached. When the Runner breaches Archives, the first candidate they choose is a piece of ice, and they apply the replacement effect from Immolation Script to trash a rezzed copy of the chosen ice instead of accessing the candidate copy. No card was actually accessed, so the Runner can proceed to choose another candidate. However, if the Runner had first accessed a card without applying Immolation Script's replacement effect, they would then be prohibited from accessing any more cards, so there would be no more candidates to choose and they could not apply a replacement effect afterward.
- <a id="rule_end_run_ends_breach"></a>**7.3.7.** An "end the run" effect that resolves while a server is being breached can sometimes end that breach. See [section 6.1.4](#rule_end_the_run).
- <a id="rule_consecutive_breaches"></a>**7.3.8.** If a server would be breached while a breach is already in progress, instead the new breach takes place when the current breach ends. The effect creating the delayed breach is treated as a conditional ability controlled by the Runner.
    - > *Example:* The Runner steals Clone Retirement during a breach of HQ, causing the Corp to take bad publicity. The Runner must complete the current breach before beginning the breach granted from Raymond Flint's first ability.

## <a id="sec_determining_candidates"></a>7.4. Determining Candidates

- <a id="rule_candidates_beginning_breach"></a>**7.4.1.** The candidates at the beginning of a breach are as follows:
    - <a id="rule_candidates_in_server_root"></a>**a.** Each card in the root of the breached server is a candidate.
    - <a id="rule_candidates_in_hq"></a>**b.** If the breached server is HQ, each card in the Corp's hand is a candidate.
    - <a id="rule_candidates_in_rnd"></a>**c.** If the breached server is R&D, the top card of the Corp's deck is a candidate.
    - <a id="rule_candidates_in_archives"></a>**d.** If the breached server is Archives, each card in the Corp's discard pile is a candidate.
- <a id="rule_prohibiting_access"></a>**7.4.2.** If at any time the Runner is prohibited from accessing an object, that object ceases to be a candidate. It cannot become a candidate as long as accessing it remains prohibited.
    - > *Example:* The Corp performs a successful trace with the ability on Ash 2X3ZB9CY, prohibiting the Runner from accessing any card other than Ash for the remainder of the current run. When the server is breached, Ash will be the only candidate for the Runner's first selection, and then there will be no candidates remaining, so the breach will end.
    - <a id="rule_prohibition_removed"></a>**a.** If an ability prohibiting some or all accesses becomes inactive during a breach, candidates are reevaluated to account for the prohibition no longer applying: for each card in or in the root of the breached server, if that object had been prohibited from being accessed by the newly inactive ability, and if no other rule or effect is applicable that would stop that object from being a candidate, then that object becomes a candidate.
        - > *Example:* The Runner breaches HQ during a run on HQ while Flagship is rezzed in the root of HQ. They have an installed copy of Docklands Pass, so the random access limit is set to 2. The Runner accesses a card in HQ, meaning that Flagship's restriction applies and all the other cards in HQ cease to be candidates. The Runner then accesses Flagship and uses Cupellation's ability to host Flagship on Cupellation, thereby uninstalling it without its persistent ability remaining active (see [section 9.12.5](#subsec_persistent)). Since the restriction on accessing cards other than Flagship no longer applies, the cards in HQ become candidates again, except for the card the Runner previously accessed.
    - <a id="rule_prohibiting_access_to_1"></a>**b.** Some abilities state that the Runner "cannot access more than 1 card" during a particular run. The effect of this ability is that as long as the Runner has accessed a card during the indicated run, they are prohibited from accessing other cards during that run. The ability has no effect on breaches or candidates until a card has been accessed, and for breaches of HQ and R&D, it never affects the random access limit, even if a card was accessed before the breach began.
        - > *Example:* The Runner breaches HQ during a run on HQ while Flagship is rezzed in the root of HQ, and they have an installed copy of Docklands Pass, but they have previously accessed a card during that run because of Otoroshi's ability. The random access limit is still set to 2, but since the Runner is not allowed to access any cards other than Flagship, none of the cards in the Corp's hand will be candidates. If Flagship's ability becomes inactive before the breach ends, the cards in the Corp's hand will become candidates and the Runner will be able to access 2 of them.
- <a id="rule_candidates_already_accessed"></a>**7.4.3.** Once the Runner chooses a candidate for access, it ceases to be a candidate for the remainder of that breach. It does not matter whether the chosen candidate is actually accessed. As long as the card is chosen, it ceases to be a candidate.
    - > *Example:* The Runner is breaching Archives during a run made with Immolation Script. They choose a piece of ice from the set of candidates, and apply Immolation Script's replacement effect to trash another card instead of accessing the chosen ice. After resolving this, the chosen ice is no longer a candidate and cannot be accessed again this breach. (The newly-trashed card, however, could be a candidate.)
    - > *Example:* The Corp is playing Gagarin Deep Space: Expanding the Horizon, and the Runner breaches a remote server. When the Runner chooses a candidate from the root of that server, an additional cost to access is imposed. The Runner declines to pay the cost and therefore does not perform the access. That card still ceases to be a candidate.
- <a id="rule_after_reaching_random_access_limit"></a>**7.4.4.** When the random access limit is reached during a breach of HQ or R&D, all candidates in the zone corresponding to the breached server cease to be candidates, and no cards in that zone can become candidates for the remainder of that breach. See [section 7.3.5](#rule_random_access_limit).
- <a id="rule_candidates_leaving_server"></a>**7.4.5.** If a candidate leaves the breached server, it ceases to be a candidate. Note that the moved card could be a new object, which could be eligible to become a new candidate if appropriate.
    - > *Example:* The Runner trashes an installed upgrade during a breach of Archives. That card moves to the Corp's discard pile. The new object for the upgrade's existence in the Corp's discard pile becomes a candidate, so the Runner will access the same physical card twice during this breach.
- <a id="rule_candidates_entering_server"></a>**7.4.6.** Cards entering a server while a breach is in progress can become candidates.
    - <a id="rule_candidates_entering_root"></a>**a.** If a card enters the root of a server during a breach of that server, the Runner decides whether that card becomes a candidate during [step 10.3.1j](#step_checkpoint_card_entering_root_during_breach) of the next checkpoint. If the Runner chooses for the card not to become a candidate, then that object cannot be a candidate for the remainder of the breach.
    - <a id="rule_candidates_entering_hq"></a>**b.** If a card enters the Corp's hand during a breach of HQ while the random access limit has not yet been reached, that card becomes a candidate.
    - <a id="rule_candidates_entering_rnd"></a>**c.** If a card enters the Corp's deck during a breach of R&D, that card could become a candidate (either at that time or later in the breach), depending on the random access limit and its position within the deck. See [section 7.4.7](#rule_rnd_candidates_1_at_a_time).
    - <a id="rule_candidates_entering_archives"></a>**d.** If a card enters the Corp's discard pile during a breach of Archives, that card becomes a candidate. See also [rule 7.3.2a](#rule_archives_facedowns_after_breach).
- <a id="rule_rnd_candidates_1_at_a_time"></a>**7.4.7.** During a breach of R&D, the Runner is presented with 1 candidate from the Corp's deck at a time in turn, working down from the top of the deck. Cards are made candidates 1 at a time until the access limit is reached.
    - <a id="rule_rnd_topmost_eligibile_candidate"></a>**a.** After the Runner chooses a candidate from the Corp's deck and whenever any cards enter, leave, or are reordered within the Corp's deck, all cards in the Corp's deck cease to be candidates. Then, if the random access limit has not been reached, the topmost eligible card in the deck becomes a candidate. A card is eligible for this purpose if the Runner has not already chosen to access that object and is not prohibited from accessing that object in this breach.
        - > *Example:* The Runner plays The Maker's Eye and breaches R&D, accessing 2 additional cards. They access Celebrity Gift and then access and steal Bacterial Programming. The Corp uses Bacterial Programming's ability to rearrange cards in R&D, but they leave Celebrity Gift as the top card of R&D. The cards returned to the top of R&D are new objects, so the Runner must continue the breach from the top of R&D and will access Celebrity Gift again.
        - > *Example:* The Corp is playing Seidr Laboratories and has Strongbox rezzed in the root of R&D. The Runner breaches R&D with a random access limit of 4. They first access the top card of the Corp's deck and leave it in place. The 2nd card from the top now becomes a candidate, and turns out to be an agenda, which the Runner steals by spending [click] to pay the additional cost imposed by Strongbox. The Corp then uses Seidr Laboratories to add 1 card from Archives to the top of R&D. The 3rd candidate presented to the Runner is the card newly added to the top of R&D. After accessing that card, the next card down is still the object that was accessed first, so the card below that (currently 3rd from the top) becomes the 4th and final candidate.
    - <a id="rule_rnd_access_from_bottom"></a>**b.** One card, Showing Off, allows the Runner to access cards from the bottom of R&D. While this effect applies, candidates in the Corp's deck are presented working up from the bottom: treat [rule 7.4.1c](#rule_candidates_in_rnd) as if it said "bottom" instead of "top" and [rule 7.4.7a](#rule_rnd_topmost_eligibile_candidate) as if it said "bottommost" instead of "topmost".

## <a id="sec_breaching_steps"></a>7.5. Steps of Breaching a Server

- <a id="step_breaching_begins"></a>**7.5.1.** Breaching the server formally begins. Conditions related to breaching this server are met.
- <a id="step_flip_archives"></a>**7.5.2.** If the breached server is Archives, turn all cards in the Corp's discard pile faceup.
- <a id="step_determine_candidates_limit"></a>**7.5.3.** If the breached server is HQ or R&D, determine the limit to how many times the Runner can choose a candidate from the Corp's hand or deck.
- <a id="step_choose_candidate"></a>**7.5.4.** The Runner chooses a candidate. If they cannot, skip to [step 7.5.7](#step_breach_complete).
- <a id="step_access_candidate"></a>**7.5.5.** The Runner accesses the chosen card.
- <a id="step_repeat_candidate_selection"></a>**7.5.6.** Return to [step 7.5.4](#step_choose_candidate).
- <a id="step_breach_complete"></a>**7.5.7.** The breach is complete. Conditions related to the breach ending are met.

# <a id="chpt_card_manipulation"></a>8. Card Manipulation

## <a id="sec_faceup_facedown_status"></a>8.1. Faceup and Facedown Status

- <a id="rule_rezzed_unrezzed"></a>**8.1.1.** An agenda, asset, upgrade, or piece of ice is **unrezzed** if it is installed and facedown. An asset, upgrade, or piece of ice is **rezzed** if it is installed and faceup. Runner cards, faceup agendas, operations, events, and counters are neither rezzed nor unrezzed.
- <a id="sec_rez"></a>**8.1.2.** To **rez** an unrezzed card is to turn it faceup.
    - <a id="rule_rez_in_paw"></a>**a.** Some paid ability windows allow the Corp to rez assets and upgrades. See [section 9.2.7](#rule_paid_ability_window). The paid ability window at [step 6.9.2b](#step_approach_paw) of a run allows the Corp to rez ice.
    - <a id="rule_rez_by_ability"></a>**b.** Card abilities can direct or allow the Corp to rez cards at other times.
    - <a id="rule_cannot_rez_agendas"></a>**c.** Agendas cannot be rezzed.
    - <a id="rule_inherent_rez_cost"></a>**d.** Before rezzing a card, the Corp must pay that card's rez cost. This includes both rezzing in a paid ability window and rezzing through a card ability. Card abilities can modify or ignore rez costs, but must state this explicitly.
    - <a id="rule_rez_procedure"></a>**e.** To resolve rezzing a card, the Corp pays its rez cost (accounting for any modifiers or additional costs), a checkpoint occurs (as required by [rule 10.3.4](#rule_checkpoint_after_paying_cost)), then the Corp turns the card faceup. For purposes of meeting trigger conditions, the card is considered to be rezzed at the conclusion of this process.
- <a id="sec_derez"></a>**8.1.3.** To **derez** a rezzed card is to turn it facedown.
    - <a id="rule_derez_by_ability"></a>**a.** Cards are only derezzed by card effects.
    - <a id="rule_derez_cost"></a>**b.** There is no inherent cost associated with derezzing a card.
    - <a id="rule_derez_procedure"></a>**c.** Derezzing a card is instantaneous and has no component steps.
- <a id="sec_facedown_runner_cards"></a>**8.1.4.** Some abilities can install Runner cards facedown or turn already-installed Runner cards facedown.
    - <a id="rule_facedown_runner_cards_are_blank"></a>**a.** Installed Runner cards that are facedown do not have any characteristics (name, card type, faction, cost, subtypes, influence, etc), and their abilities are not active.
    - <a id="rule_facedown_runner_cards_are_distinct"></a>**b.** Facedown installed Runner cards are distinct. The origin of each such card (when and how it was installed facedown, or what faceup installed card was turned facedown) is open information.
    - <a id="rule_facedown_runner_cards_flip_in_heap"></a>**c.** After a facedown Runner card enters the heap, it is turned faceup.
    - <a id="rule_flip_is_not_uninstall"></a>**d.** A Runner card turned facedown is not considered to be uninstalled and simply remains in the play area.
    - <a id="rule_unflipping_runner_cards"></a>**e.** If an installed facedown Runner card is turned faceup, the card regains its printed characteristics and it becomes active. Turning an installed facedown Runner card faceup does not meet the trigger conditions of "when installed" abilities.
    - <a id="rule_runner_cards_neither_rezzed_nor_unrezzed"></a>**f.** Runner cards are never considered rezzed or unrezzed.

## <a id="sec_card_movements"></a>8.2. Card Movements

- <a id="sec_movement"></a>**8.2.1.** A **movement** is a way that cards change their zone or location following special rules. The movements are: arrange, draw, forfeit, install, play, search, score, steal, swap, and trash.
    - <a id="rule_non_movements"></a>**a.** Other terms that change the locations of cards are not movements. If an ability directs a player to "add" or "move" cards to 1 or more locations, simply put those cards in the indicated locations. If an ability or game rule directs a player to "discard", "set aside", or "remove from the game" 1 or more cards, simply put those cards in their owner's discard pile, the set-aside zone, or the removed-from-game zone, respectively.
- <a id="sec_replacing_movements"></a>**8.2.2.** If a replacement effect modifies the effects of a movement, but does not replace the entire movement by name, the modified effect is still an occurrence of that movement and can still meet trigger conditions relating to that type of movement.
    - > *Example:* Harbinger's ability modifies the effects of trashing it by replacing adding Harbinger to the heap with turning it facedown. Harbinger is still trashed when the modified effect resolves. If this is the first time this turn that the Runner trashed one of their installed cards, they will be able to resolve Wasteland's ability.
    - <a id="rule_cancelled_movement"></a>**a.** If a movement is replaced or prevented entirely or otherwise cannot be carried out, that movement does not take place and conditions related to that type of movement are not met.
        - > *Example:* The Corp is resolving the first subroutine on Rototurret to trash an installed program. The Runner uses Sacrificial Construct to prevent the program from being trashed. Since trashing did not occur, the Runner does not place a power counter on District 99, and District 99 can still meet its trigger condition if a program or piece of hardware is successfully trashed later in the turn.
- <a id="movement_arrange"></a>**8.2.3.** To **arrange** a set of cards is to reposition them among their current locations. The full rules for arranging are detailed in [section 8.3](#sec_arranging_rearranging).
- <a id="movement_draw"></a>**8.2.4.** To **draw** a given number of cards is to move that many cards from the top of a player's deck to their hand. The full rules for drawing cards are detailed in [section 8.4](#sec_drawing_cards).
- <a id="movement_forfeit"></a>**8.2.5.** To **forfeit** an agenda is to move it from a player's score area to the removed-from-game zone. When a player forfeits an agenda, its agenda points no longer contribute to that player's score, and any objects hosted on it are trashed.
- <a id="movement_install"></a>**8.2.6.** To **install** a card is to move it from its current zone to the play area. The full rules for installing cards are detailed in [section 8.5](#sec_install_uninstall).
- <a id="movement_play"></a>**8.2.7.** To **play** an event or operation is to move it from its current zone to the play area, resolve its play abilities, and then trash it. The full rules for playing events and operations are detailed in [section 8.6](#sec_playing_events_and_operations).
- <a id="movement_search"></a>**8.2.8.** To **search** is to look at a specified zone and potentially set aside cards from that zone. The full rules for searching are detailed in [section 8.7](#sec_searching_cards).
- <a id="movement_score"></a>**8.2.9.** To **score** an agenda is to move it from its current zone to the Corp's score area. The full rules for scoring are detailed in [section 1.17](#sec_score_scoring_steal).
- <a id="movement_steal"></a>**8.2.10.** To **steal** an agenda is to move it from its current zone to the Runner's score area. The full rules for stealing are detailed in [section 1.17](#sec_score_scoring_steal).
- <a id="movement_swap"></a>**8.2.11.** To **swap** a pair of cards is to move each card to the other's location simultaneously. The full rules for swapping are detailed in [section 8.8](#sec_swapping_cards).
